Breakpoints story ok
Figma `Breakpoint` is responsive CSS, not a prop: the header is a size container. Below 768px of its own width it uses the Phone values; from 768px the Tablet ones (logo side padding 16 → 20, title label/600/md → label/600/lg).

  <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: 24, padding: 24, overflowX: 'auto' }}>
    {BREAKPOINTS.map((bp) => (
      <Labeled key={bp} label={`${bp} · ${WIDTH[bp]}px`}>
        <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: 16 }}>

            <AppHeader variant={variant} content="logo" />
          </Frame>
          <Frame width={WIDTH[bp]}>
            <AppHeader variant={variant} content="actions" title="Section title" leadingAction={SHARE} trailingAction={SHARE} />
          </Frame>

    ))}
  </div>
);

const States = () => (

    <div style={statesGrid}>

                <AppHeader variant={variant} {...layoutProps(layout)} />
              </Frame>
            ))}
          </Row>
        )),

    </div>

Docs doc ok
./components/app-header/AppHeader.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes, Controls } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as AppHeaderStories from './AppHeader.stories'

<Meta of={AppHeaderStories} />

# AppHeader

The in-app top bar: either the Daily Wire logo, or a leading action, a centered screen title and a trailing action.
It has two styles (filled or transparent), two contents (logo or actions) and adapts to Phone and Tablet widths on its own.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Header (App)` (10676:26354, page "↳ Navigation"). The actions are the DS `IconButton`.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { AppHeader } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/app-header'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<AppHeader />

<AppHeader
  content="actions"
  title="Saved episodes"
  leadingAction={{ icon: 'chevron-left', label: 'Back', onClick: goBack }}
  trailingAction={{ icon: 'share-2', label: 'Share', onClick: share }}
/>

<AppHeader variant="transparent" content="actions" leadingAction={{ icon: 'chevron-left', label: 'Back' }} />
```

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.Default} />

<Controls of={AppHeaderStories.Default} />

The defaults (`variant="filled"`, `content="logo"`) match the Figma component defaults.
AppHeader renders a `<header>`, so at the top level of a page it is the `banner` landmark.

---

## Content

Figma `Content`:

- **Logo** (`content="logo"`) — the Daily Wire lockup (`Brand / Logo`, Lockup Full), left aligned. No actions, no title.
- **Icon Button** (`content="actions"`) — a leading and a trailing `IconButton` (minimal · primary · md, 40px) with the title centered on the bar.
  `leadingAction`, `trailingAction` and `title` are used only with this content.

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.Content} />

---

## Variants

Figma `Style`:

- **Filled** — `--color-background-neutral-bold-default` surface, follows the theme.
- **Transparent** (Figma `WithoutBg`) — no fill: for screens where the header sits on the content (artwork, hero media). The logo, title and icons keep their neutral-bold colors, so check contrast against what is behind.

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.Variants} />

---

## Breakpoints

Figma `Breakpoint` is not a prop. The header is a CSS size container and reads **its own width**:
below 768px it uses the Phone values, from 768px the Tablet ones.

| | Phone | Tablet (≥ 768px) |
|---|---|---|
| Logo padding | `--spacing-xl` (16) all sides | `--spacing-xl` (16) top / bottom · `--spacing-2xl` (20) sides |
| Actions padding | `--spacing-md` (8) all sides | `--spacing-md` (8) all sides |
| Title | `label/600/md` (`--font-size-sm`, 14px) | `label/600/lg` (`--font-size-md`, 16px) |

Logo bar: 16 + 12 + 16 = 44px. Actions bar: 8 + 40 + 8 = 56px (`min-height: --size-4xl`, kept when actions are hidden).

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Actions and title

Figma `Show Leading Action`, `Show Trailing Action` and `Show Text` map to passing or omitting `leadingAction`, `trailingAction` and `title`.
Each action is `{ icon, label, onClick?, disabled? }`: `label` becomes the icon button's `aria-label`.
Both side columns keep at least the icon button width, so the title stays centered when one action is hidden.
Long titles wrap (balanced) instead of being cut, so zoom and the browser minimum font size keep them readable.

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.Actions} />

---

## Inspect

Pick style, content, breakpoint, actions and text. The header is drawn with its real measurements (Phone at 1×, Tablet at 0.6×); the panel lists the tokens that apply — hover a token to see where it acts.
Leading, Trailing and Text apply to `content="actions"`.

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

The header itself has no interactive state. This grid shows every Style × Content × leading / trailing / text combination at both breakpoints,
then every state of the actions (default, hover, pressed, focus, disabled). Action colors, focus ring and touch target come from `IconButton`; hover, pressed and focus are forced with `data-state`.

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.States} />

---

## Accessibility

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| Landmark | `<header>`: the `banner` landmark when it is not inside `<article>` / `<section>` / `<main>` |
| Title | `<h1>` by default (it names the screen); `titleAs="h2"` or `"span"` when the page already has its own `h1` |
| Logo | Inline SVG with `role="img"` and `aria-label` (`logoLabel`, default "Daily Wire") |
| Actions | `IconButton`: native `<button>`, `aria-label` from `label`, focus ring, 44px hit area on touch screens |
| Zoom | No fixed height (`min-height` only); the title wraps instead of clipping |

---

## Tokens

Every property of `AppHeader.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar). Action tokens are listed in IconButton.

<Canvas of={AppHeaderStories.Tokens} />

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={AppHeaderStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `'filled' \| 'transparent'` | `'filled'` | Figma `Style` (Filled / WithoutBg) |
| `content` | `'logo' \| 'actions'` | `'logo'` | Figma `Content` (Logo / Icon Button) |
| `title` | `ReactNode` | — | Centered title (Figma `Text`, `Show Text`); `content="actions"` only |
| `titleAs` | `'h1' \| 'h2' \| 'span'` | `'h1'` | Element used for the title |
| `leadingAction` | `AppHeaderAction` | — | Left icon button (Figma `Show Leading Action`); `content="actions"` only |
| `trailingAction` | `AppHeaderAction` | — | Right icon button (Figma `Show Trailing Action`); `content="actions"` only |
| `logoLabel` | `string` | `'Daily Wire'` | Accessible name of the logo |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

`AppHeaderAction`: `{ icon: IconName; label: string; onClick?: MouseEventHandler<HTMLButtonElement>; disabled?: boolean; [data-*]: string }`.

> Also accepts native `<header>` attributes via spread (`id`, `style`, `aria-*`, etc.). The ref points to the `<header>`.

States story ok
Every store and style in every state. Figma draws one state; store guidelines do not allow recoloring the badge, so hover / pressed look like default. Focus adds the DS ring, disabled dims the artwork with `--opacity-disabled`. Hover / pressed / focus are forced with `data-state`.
const States = () => (
  <div style={{ display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: `auto repeat(${STATES.length}, 1fr)`, gap: '16px 12px', padding: 24, alignItems: 'center', fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)' }}>
    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
    ))}
    {APP_STORE_BADGE_STYLES.map((badgeStyle) =>
      APP_STORE_BADGE_STORES.map((store) => (
        <Row key={`${badgeStyle}-${store}`}>
          <div>{store} · {badgeStyle}</div>
          {STATES.map((s) => (
            <Cell key={s}>
              <AppStoreBadge
                store={store}
                badgeStyle={badgeStyle}
                href="#"
                disabled={s === 'disabled'}
                {...(FORCED.includes(s) ? { 'data-state': s } : {})}
              />
            </Cell>
          ))}
        </Row>
      )),
    )}
  </div>
);

Docs doc ok
./components/app-store-badge/AppStoreBadge.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as AppStoreBadgeStories from './AppStoreBadge.stories'

<Meta of={AppStoreBadgeStories} />

# App Store Badge

Official "Download on …" badge for each store where the app ships, rendered as a link.
Its accessible name is the badge's own text (for example **"Download on the App Store"**); pass `aria-label` to change it.
Spec reviewed against Figma `App Store Buttons` (7761:19420), axes `Company` × `Style` (Preferred / Alternative).

The artwork is **not redrawn**: each badge is the Figma export, stored in `icons/store-badges/<store>-<style>.svg` and loaded as a file (not through SVGR), so the brand colors are never replaced with `currentColor`.
Store guidelines do not allow changing the badge, so it does not follow the Light/Dark theme — pick `preferred` (dark badge) or `alternative` (light badge) for the surface it sits on.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { AppStoreBadge } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/app-store-badge'

<AppStoreBadge store="google-play" href="https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=…" target="_blank" />
```

The SVG files are imported as assets; the consumer bundler must resolve `*.svg` imports to URLs (Vite and webpack 5 asset modules do this by default).
With `target="_blank"` the link gets `rel="noopener noreferrer"` unless you pass `rel`.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={AppStoreBadgeStories.Default} />

<Canvas of={AppStoreBadgeStories.DownloadRow} />

---

## Variants

Eight stores (`store`) × two styles (`badgeStyle`):

| `store` | Accessible name |
|---|---|
| `app-store` | Download on the App Store |
| `mac-app-store` | Download on the Mac App Store |
| `amazon-appstore` | Available at Amazon Appstore |
| `galaxy-store` | Available on Galaxy Store |
| `huawei-appgallery` | Explore it on AppGallery |
| `f-droid` | Get it on F-Droid |
| `google-play` | Get it on Google Play |
| `microsoft-store` | Get it from Microsoft |

<Canvas of={AppStoreBadgeStories.AllBadges} />

---

## Size

40px tall (`--size-2xl`), width from the artwork (112–156px). An invisible area extends the touch target to `--touch-target-min` (44px).

---

## States

Figma draws one state. Hover and pressed keep the artwork unchanged (store guidelines); focus adds the DS ring; disabled removes the link and dims the artwork with `--opacity-disabled`.
Hover, pressed and focus are forced for review:

<Canvas of={AppStoreBadgeStories.States} />

### Focus

Keyboard focus shows the DS ring: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset`, following the badge's 8px corners (`--border-radius-md`).

---

## Inspect

Pick store, style and state. The badge is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={AppStoreBadgeStories.Inspect} />

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={AppStoreBadgeStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `store` | `'app-store' \| 'mac-app-store' \| 'amazon-appstore' \| 'galaxy-store' \| 'huawei-appgallery' \| 'f-droid' \| 'google-play' \| 'microsoft-store'` | `'app-store'` | Store (Figma `Company`) |
| `badgeStyle` | `'preferred' \| 'alternative'` | `'preferred'` | Dark (preferred) or light (alternative) badge (Figma `Style`) |
| `href` | `string` | — | Store listing URL |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Renders the badge without a link, dimmed |
| `aria-label` | `string` | badge text | Accessible name |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<a>` attributes via spread (`target`, `rel`, `onClick`, etc.).

ArticleBlock

components-articleblock · ./components/article-block/ArticleBlock.stories.tsx
The text block of an article: title, description, metadata row and a "more" action. With `href` the title link is stretched over the block (or its card), so the whole block is one link; the "more" button and the metadata action sit above it, outside the `<a>`.
Prop types (react-docgen) 17 prop types 
Component: components/article-block/ArticleBlock.tsx::ArticleBlock
Props:
/**
 * Forces a state for docs / QA. Real hover and press come from pointing at the block (the stretched link).
 */
data-state?: 'hover' | 'pressed' | 'focus'

/**
 * Standfirst under the title (body/400/md on `xl`, body/400/sm otherwise)
 */
description?: ReactNode

/**
 * Title element: a heading level for the document outline (default `h3`)
 */
headingAs?: (typeof ARTICLE_HEADING_ELEMENTS)[number] = 'h3'

/**
 * Article URL. The title becomes the block's one link, stretched over it (see `linkArea`).
 */
href?: string

/**
 * What the stretched title link covers: this block, or the nearest positioned ancestor (a card). Default `block`.
 */
linkArea?: (typeof ARTICLE_BLOCK_LINK_AREAS)[number] = 'block'

/**
 * Extra attributes for the title `<a>` (`target`, `rel`, `onClick`, `aria-*`…)
 */
linkProps?: ArticleHeadingProps['linkProps']

/**
 * The metadata row (`ArticleMetadata` props). `size` defaults to `sm` and `variant` to the Figma type of the
 * block size (`default` on xl / lg, `article` on md / sm / xsm). Leave `authorHref` out unless the author link
 * should be a separate stop: it then sits above the card link.
 */
metadata?: ArticleMetadataProps

/**
 * Extra `IconButton` props for the "more" button (`aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`, `disabled`…)
 */
moreButtonProps?: Omit<IconButtonProps, 'icon' | 'aria-label' | 'size' | 'type' | 'buttonStyle'>

/**
 * Accessible name of the "more" button
 */
moreLabel?: string = 'More options'

/**
 * Called by the "more" (vertical dots) button. It sits above the card link and is not inside it.
 */
onMoreClick?: MouseEventHandler<HTMLButtonElement>

/**
 * Figma `Show Description`
 */
showDescription?: boolean = true

/**
 * Figma `Show Heading title`: the title row (title + "more" button)
 */
showHeading?: boolean = true

/**
 * Figma `Show Metadata`
 */
showMetadata?: boolean = true

/**
 * Figma `Show dots`: the "more" button
 */
showMore?: boolean = true

/**
 * xl 32 · lg 24 · md 20 · sm 16 · xsm 14 px title; also sets gaps, description size and the "more" button size
 */
size?: (typeof ARTICLE_BLOCK_SIZES)[number] = 'xl'

/**
 * Article title (rendered with `ArticleHeading`)
 */
title?: ReactNode

/**
 * `exclusive` = serif title (Figma `…/exclusive` text styles), used by exclusive article cards
 */
tone?: (typeof ARTICLE_HEADING_TONES)[number] = 'default'

import { Fragment } from "react";
Default story ok

const Default = () => <ArticleBlock

Sizes story ok
xl 32 · lg 24 (semibold titles) and md 20 · sm 16 · xsm 14 (medium titles). Gaps, the description size and the "more" button follow the size; the metadata row is Figma type `default` on xl / lg and `article` below.
const Sizes = (args) => (
  <ShowcaseBox direction="column" style={{ alignItems: 'stretch', gap: 48 }}>
    {[...ARTICLE_BLOCK_SIZES].reverse().map((size) => (
      <ArticleBlock key={size} {...args} size={size} metadata={figmaMetadata(size)} />
    ))}
  </ShowcaseBox>
);

States story ok
The full Figma set (15 variants: size × state) plus keyboard focus (the title link's ring). Hover, pressed and focus are forced with `data-state`.
const States = (args) => (
  <div
    style={{
      display: 'grid',
      gridTemplateColumns: `auto repeat(${STATES.length}, ${COLUMN_WIDTH}px)`,
      gap: '40px 32px',
      padding: 24,
      alignItems: 'start',
      fontSize: 12,
      color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)',
    }}
  >
    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
    ))}
    {[...ARTICLE_BLOCK_SIZES].reverse().map((size) => (
      <Fragment key={size}>
        <div>{size}</div>
        {STATES.map((s) => (
          <ArticleBlock key={s} {...args} size={size} metadata={figmaMetadata(size)} {...stateProps(s)} />
        ))}
      </Fragment>
    ))}
  </div>
);

Docs doc ok
./components/article-block/ArticleBlock.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as ArticleBlockStories from './ArticleBlock.stories'

<Meta of={ArticleBlockStories} />

# ArticleBlock

The text block of an article: title, description, metadata row and a "more" (vertical dots) action. It is the text part of article cards and lists.
Five sizes and three states, plus a flag per part.
Spec from Figma `ArticleBlock` (8214:53232), section "Article" on page "↳ Content". It nests the DS `ArticleHeading`, `ArticleMetadata` and `IconButton`.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { ArticleBlock } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/article-block'

<ArticleBlock
  size="md"
  title="FBI Alert Warns Iran May Eye Drone Attack On American Soil"
  href="/news/fbi-alert-iran-drones"
  description="The FBI recently warned California authorities…"
  metadata={{ author: 'Jennie Taer', publishDate: new Date(article.publishedAt), readTime: 5, showEpisodeNumber: false }}
  onMoreClick={openArticleMenu}
/>
```

Renders a `<div>` with the title (`ArticleHeading`, `h3` by default), a `<p>` description and the metadata row.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.Default} />

---

## One link per block

With `href`, the title is the block's only link and its hit area is **stretched** over the whole block (the accessible card pattern): pointing at or pressing anywhere on the block drives the title's hover and pressed colors, and screen readers hear one link named by the title.

- The **"more" button** (`IconButton`, `dots-vertical`) and the metadata **action** (Listen) are separate buttons. They are not inside the `<a>`; they sit above the stretched link (`z-index: 1`) and stay clickable.
- `linkArea="container"` stretches the link over the nearest positioned ancestor instead of the block, so a card that also holds an image is one link (`ArticleCard` does this).
- Without `href` the block is static text.

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.LinkAreaContainer} />

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.WithoutLink} />

---

## Sizes

| Size | Title (`ArticleHeading`) | Description | Gap: headline ↔ metadata | Gap: title ↔ description | "More" button | Metadata type |
|------|------|------|------|------|------|------|
| `xl` | `xl` semibold (`heading/600/lg`, 32) | `body/400/md` (16) | `--spacing-3xl` | `--spacing-xl` | `md` (40) | `default` |
| `lg` | `lg` semibold (`heading/600/md`, 24) | `body/400/sm` (14) | `--spacing-3xl` | `--spacing-xl` | `md` (40) | `default` |
| `md` | `md` medium (`heading/500/sm`, 20) | `body/400/sm` | `--spacing-lg` | `--spacing-xl` | `sm` (32) | `article` |
| `sm` | `sm` medium (`heading/500/xs`, 16) | `body/400/sm` | `--spacing-lg` | `--spacing-xl` | `sm` (32) | `article` |
| `xsm` | `xsm` medium (`title/500/sm`, 14) | `body/400/sm` | `--spacing-sm` | `--spacing-sm` | `sm` (32) | `article` |

The title and the "more" button share a top-aligned row with `--spacing-md` between them. The description is `text-neutral-subtlest-default` at a 150% line height and wraps freely. The metadata row is `ArticleMetadata` size `sm`; its `variant` follows the table unless `metadata.variant` is set. In Figma the Listen action is on for `xl` and `lg`: pass `metadata.action` for it.

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.Sizes} />

---

## Parts

The Figma booleans: `showHeading` (Figma `Show Heading title`: the title row, with the dots), `showMore` (`Show dots`), `showDescription`, `showMetadata`. A part also needs content to render (`title`, `description`, `metadata`).

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.ShowToggles} />

---

## Exclusive

`tone="exclusive"` gives the title the serif `…/exclusive` text style (Libre Caslon Text, tight tracking). It is not a Figma axis of this set: exclusive `ArticleCard`s override the nested title this way.

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.Exclusive} />

---

## Inspect

Pick size and state. The block is drawn with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply. Hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

All 15 Figma variants (size × state) plus keyboard focus; hover, pressed and focus are forced for review:

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.States} />

| State | Title | Description, metadata, dots |
|---|---|---|
| Default | `text-neutral-bold-default` | unchanged |
| Hovered | `text-neutral-bold-hovered` | unchanged |
| Pressed | `text-neutral-bold-pressed` | unchanged |
| Focus | `text-neutral-bold-default` + `focus-info-bold-default` ring around the title | unchanged |

The color change uses `--duration-slow` with `--easing-ease-out` (Figma: on hover, dissolve 300ms ease-out) and is turned off under `prefers-reduced-motion`. Focus has no Figma variant in this set: it is the title link's ring from `ArticleHeading`. The "more" button and the Listen action keep their own `IconButton` / `LabeledIconButton` states.

---

## Tokens

Every property of `ArticleBlock.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar). Title colors are `ArticleHeading`'s; the row and its action use `ArticleMetadata` and `LabeledIconButton` tokens.

<Canvas of={ArticleBlockStories.Tokens} />

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={ArticleBlockStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `title` | `ReactNode` | — | Article title |
| `href` | `string` | — | Article URL: the title becomes the block's stretched link |
| `description` | `ReactNode` | — | Standfirst under the title |
| `metadata` | `ArticleMetadataProps` | — | Metadata row (`size` `sm`; `variant` by block size) |
| `size` | `'xsm' \| 'sm' \| 'md' \| 'lg' \| 'xl'` | `'xl'` | See Sizes |
| `tone` | `'default' \| 'exclusive'` | `'default'` | Serif title for exclusive content |
| `headingAs` | `'h1'`–`'h6' \| 'p' \| 'span'` | `'h3'` | Title element |
| `linkProps` | `AnchorHTMLAttributes` | — | Extra attributes for the title `<a>` |
| `linkArea` | `'block' \| 'container'` | `'block'` | What the stretched link covers |
| `onMoreClick` | `MouseEventHandler` | — | "More" button click |
| `moreLabel` | `string` | `'More options'` | Accessible name of the "more" button |
| `moreButtonProps` | `Partial<IconButtonProps>` | — | `aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded`, `disabled`… |
| `showHeading` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Heading title` |
| `showMore` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show dots` |
| `showDescription` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Description` |
| `showMetadata` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Metadata` |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class on the root |

> Also accepts native `<div>` attributes via spread (`id`, `aria-*`, `data-*`, etc.).

Every type in every Figma state, at the size picked in Controls. Hover, pressed and focused are forced with `data-state`.
const States = (args) => (
  <div style={gridStyle(STATES.length)}>
    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s === 'focus' ? 'focused' : s}</div>

Figma Matrix story ok
The full Figma set (36 variants): state × type rows, size columns.
const FigmaMatrix = (args) => (
  <div style={gridStyle(ARTICLE_CARD_SIZES.length)}>
    <div />
    {ARTICLE_CARD_SIZES.map((size) => (
      <div key={size}>{size}</div>
    ))}
    {STATES.flatMap((s) =>
      ARTICLE_CARD_VARIANTS.map((variant) => (
        <Fragment key={`${s}-${variant}`}>
          <div style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{`${s === 'focus' ? 'focused' : s} · ${variant}`}</div>
          {ARTICLE_CARD_SIZES.map((size) => (
            <ArticleCard key={size} {...args} variant={variant} size={size} title={FIGMA_TITLES[size]} {...stateProps(s)} />
          ))}
        </Fragment>
      )),
    )}
  </div>
);

Docs doc ok
./components/article-card/ArticleCard.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as ArticleCardStories from './ArticleCard.stories'

<Meta of={ArticleCardStories} />

# ArticleCard

An article teaser for feeds and grids: an image with an optional badge, the title, the metadata row and a "more" action. The whole card is one link.
Three types, three sizes and four states, plus `Show Badge`.
Spec from Figma `Article Card` (8214:53996), section "Article" on page "↳ Content". It nests the DS `ArticleBlock` (with `ArticleHeading`, `ArticleMetadata` › `Byline`, `IconButton`) and `Badge`.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { ArticleCard } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/article-card'

<ArticleCard
  size="md"
  title="FBI Alert Warns Iran May Eye Drone Attack On American Soil"
  href="/news/fbi-alert-iran-drones"
  image={{ src: article.imageUrl, alt: '' }}
  badge="Breaking"
  metadata={{ author: 'Jennie Taer', publishDate: new Date(article.publishedAt), readTime: 5, showEpisodeNumber: false }}
  onMoreClick={openArticleMenu}
/>
```

Renders an `<article>`: the image (`<img>` in a frame with the badge), then an `ArticleBlock` with the title (`h3` by default) and the metadata row.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={ArticleCardStories.Default} />

---

## One link per card

The title is the card's only link, and its hit area is **stretched** over the whole card (`ArticleBlock` with `linkArea="container"`): pointing at the image, the badge or the text drives the title's hover and pressed colors, and screen readers hear one link named by the title.

- The **"more" button** and a metadata **action** are separate buttons, not inside the `<a>`. They sit above the stretched link and stay clickable.
- **Keyboard focus** lands on the title link and draws the ring around the whole card (see States).
- The image usually repeats the title: give it `alt: ''`. Describe it only when it adds information.

<Canvas of={ArticleCardStories.Feed} />

---

## Types

Figma's `type` axis is the `variant` prop.

| Type | Image | Title |
|------|-------|-------|
| `default` | 16:9 (Figma 301 × 169) | `--default` family |
| `upstream` | 4:3 (Figma 301 × 226) | `--default` family |
| `exclusive` | 4:3 | `--exclusive` serif family, `--letter-spacing-tight` (`…/exclusive` text styles) |

<Canvas of={ArticleCardStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

| Size | Nested `ArticleBlock` | Title |
|------|------|------|
| `sm` | `xsm` | `title/500/sm` (`--font-size-sm`, 14) |
| `md` | `sm` | `heading/500/xs` (`--font-size-md`, 16) |
| `lg` | `md` | `heading/500/sm` (`--font-size-lg`, 20) |

The image and the text block are `--spacing-xl` apart. The image fills the card width with `--border-radius-sm` corners and `object-fit: cover`. The card has no fixed width: it fills its column (Figma draws it at 301px). The description is off in every Figma variant; the "more" button is a 32px minimal `IconButton`; the metadata row is `ArticleMetadata` type `article`, size `sm`.

<Canvas of={ArticleCardStories.Sizes} />

---

## Badge

Figma's `Show Badge`: a `Badge` (brand, `md`, regular, square) `--spacing-xl` from the image's top-left corner. Pass the label as `badge`; `badgeProps` overrides the rest (`statusDot`, `leadingIcon`, `variant`). The badge lives on the image, so a card without `image` has no badge.

<Canvas of={ArticleCardStories.ShowToggles} />

---

## Inspect

Pick type, state, size and badge. The card is drawn with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply. Hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={ArticleCardStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every type in every Figma state (hover, pressed and focused are forced for review; pick the size in Controls):

<Canvas of={ArticleCardStories.States} />

| State | Title | Card |
|---|---|---|
| Default | `text-neutral-bold-default` | — |
| Hover | `text-neutral-bold-hovered` | — |
| Pressed | `text-neutral-bold-pressed` | — |
| Focused | `text-neutral-bold-default` | `background-neutral-bold-default` fill, `--border-radius-sm`, `--focus-ring-width` ring in `focus-info-bold-default` at `--focus-ring-offset` |

The title color change uses `--duration-slow` with `--easing-ease-out` and is turned off under `prefers-reduced-motion`. On focus the title link's own ring is replaced by the card ring. The "more" button keeps its own `IconButton` states.

---

Article title text. Pass `href` to make it the card's link; without `href` it is plain text that still picks up hover / pressed colors when it sits inside a hovered or pressed `<a>` (a card that is one link).

./components/article-heading/ArticleHeading.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as ArticleHeadingStories from './ArticleHeading.stories'

<Meta of={ArticleHeadingStories} />

# ArticleHeading

The title of an article, as it appears on cards and article pages. It is usually the card's link text, so it has hover, pressed and focus states; without `href` it renders as plain text.
Five sizes, two weights, two tones and four states.
Spec from Figma `Heading-Article-Content` (7958:8107, doc frame 9022:21077), section "Article" on page "↳ Content".
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { ArticleHeading } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/article-heading'

<ArticleHeading as="h3" size="md" href="/news/oil-prices">
  Oil Prices Plummet, Stocks Climb After Trump Update On Iran War
</ArticleHeading>
```

Renders the element named by `as` (default `h3`; any heading level, `p` or `span`). With `href`, the text is wrapped in a native `<a>` that fills the title block.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={ArticleHeadingStories.Default} />

---

## Tone

Figma's `tone` axis. `exclusive` marks Daily Wire exclusive content by typography alone: no prefix, label or color change.

- **Default** — `--default` family (Libre Franklin), `--letter-spacing-default`.
- **Exclusive** — `--exclusive` family (Libre Caslon Text, serif), `--letter-spacing-tight` (−2%). Text styles `…/exclusive`.

<Canvas of={ArticleHeadingStories.Tones} />

---

## Sizes

Every size uses a 120% line height (`--line-height-120`). The text wraps; the component has no fixed height.

| Size | Text style | Font size |
|------|-----------|-----------|
| `xl` | `heading/600/lg` · `heading/500/lg` | `--font-size-2xl` (32) |
| `lg` | `heading/600/md` · `heading/500/md` | `--font-size-xl` (24) |
| `md` | `heading/600/sm` · `heading/500/sm` | `--font-size-lg` (20) |
| `sm` | `heading/600/xs` · `heading/500/xs` | `--font-size-md` (16) |
| `xsm` | `title/600/sm` · `title/500/sm` | `--font-size-sm` (14) |

<Canvas of={ArticleHeadingStories.Sizes} />

---

## Weight

`semibold` uses the `*/600/*` text styles (`--Semibold`), `medium` uses `*/500/*` (`--Medium`).

<Canvas of={ArticleHeadingStories.Weights} />

---

## In a card

An article card that navigates should have **one** link. Two ways to do it:

1. **Stretched title link** — give the heading `href` and `stretched`, and the card `position: relative`. The link's hit area covers the card, so hovering anywhere on it drives the title's hover color. Secondary actions in the card (Listen, Save…) stay clickable when they sit above it with `position: relative; z-index: 1`, and they are not nested inside the `<a>`.
2. **Card wrapped in an `<a>`** — render the heading without `href`. It still follows the link: hover and press on any ancestor `<a>` switch it to the hovered and pressed colors.

<Canvas of={ArticleHeadingStories.StretchedInCard} />

Without `href` and outside a link, the title is plain text:

<Canvas of={ArticleHeadingStories.WithoutLink} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus draws `--focus-ring-width` in `--color-focus-info-bold-default` around the whole title block, with a `--focus-ring-offset` gap and `--border-radius-sm` corners. The text keeps its default color.

---

# ArticleMetadata

The row of facts under an article title: an optional action (Listen), the author, the publish date, the read time and the episode number, separated by bullets.
Four types, two sizes, the action on or off, and a `show*` flag per item.
Spec from Figma `Metadata` (8210:45735, doc frame 8413:61610), section "Article" on page "↳ Content". It nests the DS `LabeledIconButton` (Figma "Libeled Icon Button") and `Byline`.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { ArticleMetadata } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/article-metadata'

<ArticleMetadata
  variant="feed"
  action={{ icon: 'player-play-filled', label: 'Listen', onClick: play }}
  author="Ben Shapiro"
  publishDate={new Date(article.publishedAt)}
  readTime={6}
  episodeNumber={2381}
/>
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={ArticleMetadataStories.Default} />

---

## Types

Figma's `Type` axis is the `variant` prop. Colors are identical in every type: the type sets the **order** of the items. The action, when present, always comes first.

| Type | Order |
|------|-------|
| `default` | read time · episode · date · author |
| `feed` (default) | date · read time · episode · author |
| `inverted` | author · episode · date · read time |
| `article` | author · episode · read time · date |

In `feed` without an action the date leads the row and is emphasized: `label/500` (`--Medium`) in `text-neutral-bold-default`.

<Canvas of={ArticleMetadataStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

| Size | Items | Action label | Height |
|------|-------|--------------|--------|
| `sm` | `label/400/sm` (`--font-size-xs`, 12) | `label/600/sm` | 12 · 32 with action |
| `md` | `label/400/md` (`--font-size-sm`, 14) | `label/600/md` | 14 · 32 with action |

Items and bullets are `text-neutral-subtlest-default`, `--spacing-sm` (6px) apart, vertically centered. The row wraps when it runs out of width.

<Canvas of={ArticleMetadataStories.Sizes} />

---

## Action

Figma's `Libeled Icon Button` On/Off is the `action` prop: pass `{ icon, label, onClick }` (any `LabeledIconButton` prop) for On, omit it for Off. Figma's default is `player-play-filled` + "Listen".

<Canvas of={ArticleMetadataStories.WithoutAction} />

---

## Items and data

- **Author** — `author` renders a secondary `Byline` at the row size. Add `authorHref` to make it a link; omit it when the metadata sits inside a card that is already one link. `bylineProps` passes the rest (`showAvatar`, `avatar`, `disabled`…).
- **Publish date** — rendered as `<time>`. A `Date` is formatted with `formatDate` (default "Nov 5, 2026", en-US) and fills `dateTime` with its ISO string; a string ("1 day ago") is shown as is, with `dateTime` set by the prop of that name.
- **Read time** — a number renders as "6 min"; a string is shown as is.
- **Episode number** — a number renders as "Ep. 2381"; a string is shown as is.

In a card whose title link is stretched over it (`ArticleHeading` with `stretched`), the action and a linked author sit above that link (`z-index: 1`) and stay clickable; a static author lets clicks through to the card link.

An item renders only when its `show*` flag is on (Figma `Show Author`, `Show Publish Date`, `Show ReadTime`, `Show Ep Number`; all on by default) **and** it has a value. Bullets follow the visible items and are hidden from assistive technology.

<Canvas of={ArticleMetadataStories.ShowToggles} />

<Canvas of={ArticleMetadataStories.WithData} />

---

<Canvas of={ArticleMetadataStories.Inspect} />

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={ArticleMetadataStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `'default' \| 'feed' \| 'inverted' \| 'article'` | `'feed'` | Figma `Type`: order of the items |
| `size` | `'sm' \| 'md'` | `'sm'` | 12 or 14 px items |
| `action` | `LabeledIconButtonProps` (`icon`, `label`, `onClick`…) | — | Leading action; omit for Off |
| `author` | `string` | — | Author name (Byline) |
| `authorHref` | `string` | — | Author page; omit for static text |
| `bylineProps` | `Partial<BylineProps>` | — | `showAvatar`, `avatar`, `disabled`… |
| `publishDate` | `string \| Date` | — | Shown in a `<time>` |
| `dateTime` | `string` | ISO of a `Date` | `<time dateTime>` |
| `formatDate` | `(date: Date) => string` | "Nov 5, 2026" | Formats a `Date` `publishDate` |
| `readTime` | `string \| number` | — | Number → "6 min" |
| `episodeNumber` | `string \| number` | — | Number → "Ep. 50" |
| `showAuthor` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Author` |
| `showPublishDate` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Publish Date` |
| `showReadTime` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show ReadTime` |
| `showEpisodeNumber` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Ep Number` |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class on the root |

> Also accepts native `<div>` attributes via spread (`id`, `aria-*`, `data-*`, etc.).

./components/article-video/ArticleVideo.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as ArticleVideoStories from './ArticleVideo.stories'

<Meta of={ArticleVideoStories} />

# ArticleVideo

A video inside an article or a feed card: a native `<video>` (no third-party player) with the overlay controls Figma draws, a mute toggle in the top-right corner and a play / pause button in the center.
Spec from Figma `Video Article Resource` (9149:64325) in the "Article" section of the "↳ Content" page: `VideoState` Played | Paused × `InteractionState` Default | Hovered × `AudioState` Muted | Unmuted (8 variants).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark; the overlay controls look the same in both themes.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { ArticleVideo } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/article-video'

<ArticleVideo
  src="/media/clip.mp4"
  poster="/media/clip.jpg"
  label="Explosions light up the skyline"
  autoPlay
  loop
>
  <track kind="captions" src="/media/clip.en.vtt" srcLang="en" label="English" default />
</ArticleVideo>
```

Built from DS parts: two `IconButton`s (type `translucid`, size `lg`).

---

## Usage

The docs have no sample video file, so the stories show the **poster** (the paused picture). The mute toggle works; play has nothing to start. Hover the picture to see the play button.

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.Default} />

---

## Video state

Figma `VideoState` maps to `playing` (controlled) or to the real playback state (uncontrolled). The centered button shows **play** while paused and **pause** while playing. A click on the picture also plays / pauses, as in the Figma prototype.

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.VideoState} />

## Audio state

Figma `AudioState` maps to `muted` (controlled) or `defaultMuted` (uncontrolled, `true` by default: browsers only autoplay muted video). The toggle shows `volume-3` when muted and `volume` when sound is on.

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.AudioState} />

## Interaction state

Figma `InteractionState=Hovered` shows the centered button, with a 600 ms ease-out dissolve (`--duration-slower` is the closest token). In code it appears on hover, when a control inside has keyboard focus, and, on touch screens (no hover), whenever the video is paused. `interactionState="hovered"` forces it on.

---

## Aspect

The master component is 864 × 547; the Figma instances resize it to 16:9, 1:1, 9:16 and 2:3 (Card Feed, Card Feed Ad) and stretch it to the slide in Live Updates. `aspect` sets the box ratio (default `16/9`); `fill` takes the parent's width and height. The picture always covers the box (Figma video fill: crop → `object-fit: cover`). `rounded` adds the `--border-radius-md` corners the card instances use.

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.Aspects} />

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.Rounded} />

---

## Controlled

Pass `muted` + `onMutedChange` and / or `playing` + `onPlayingChange` to own the state. When a play request fails (autoplay policy, no playable source), `onPlayingChange(false)` reports it.

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.Controlled} />

---

## Inspect

Pick VideoState, InteractionState and AudioState. The video is drawn at half the Figma frame size with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply.

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

All 8 Figma variants. Hovered is forced with `interactionState`:

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `ArticleVideo.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme. The nested `IconButton` (translucid · lg) documents its own tokens.

<Canvas of={ArticleVideoStories.Tokens} />

---

## Accessibility

- The root is a `group` named by `label` (default "Video"); use the clip title.
- No native controls: the mute toggle ("Mute", `aria-pressed` = muted) and the play / pause button ("Play" / "Pause") are real buttons with names (`muteLabel`, `playLabel`, `pauseLabel`). The center button stays in the tab order and appears when it gets keyboard focus.
- Captions: pass `<track kind="captions" … default />` as children. Without native controls there is no captions menu, so mark the track `default` to show it.
- `autoPlay` is skipped when the user prefers reduced motion; autoplaying video should stay muted.
- Both buttons are 48 px (≥ `--touch-target-min`) and show the DS focus ring.

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={ArticleVideoStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `src` | `string` | — | Video URL (or pass `<source>` children) |
| `poster` | `string` | — | Still image before playback |
| `label` | `string` | `'Video'` | Accessible name of the player group |
| `muted` · `defaultMuted` · `onMutedChange` | `boolean` · `boolean` · `(muted) => void` | — · `true` · — | Figma `AudioState` |
| `playing` · `autoPlay` · `onPlayingChange` | `boolean` · `boolean` · `(playing) => void` | — · `false` · — | Figma `VideoState` |
| `aspect` | `'16/9' \| '1/1' \| '9/16' \| '2/3' \| 'fill'` | `'16/9'` | Box ratio; `fill` follows the parent |
| `rounded` | `boolean` | `false` | `--border-radius-md` corners |
| `interactionState` | `'default' \| 'hovered'` | `'default'` | Figma `InteractionState`: force the center button visible |
| `muteLabel` · `playLabel` · `pauseLabel` | `string` | `'Mute'` · `'Play'` · `'Pause'` | Button names |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | `<track>` / `<source>` elements |
| `className` · `style` | — | — | Root box |

> `ref` points at the `<video>` element. Other native `<video>` attributes (`loop`, `preload` (default `metadata`), `playsInline` (default `true`), `crossOrigin`, `onTimeUpdate`, `onEnded`…) are forwarded to it.

 * Figma `Initials` text (1–2 characters). Drawn by `type="initials"`, and by image types when `src`

 * Figma `Size`: 4xsm 20 · 3xsm 24 · 2xsm 32 · xsm 40 · sm 48 · md 56 · lg 64 · xl 72 · 2xl 80 · 3xl 96 · 4xl 100 px
 */

 */

/**
 * Figma `Type`. `host`, `person` and `show` draw `src`; `show` is a rounded square, every other type a circle.

# Avatar

A round (or, for shows, rounded-square) picture that stands for a profile, a host, a person or a show: in account menus, profile switchers, host lists and show rows.
It has five types, eleven sizes and a selected ring. Image types fall back to initials, then to the Daily Wire mark, when there is no picture.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Avatar` (219:11928, doc section 10:1812, page "↳ Profile & Hosts"). Figma's usage note: the profile picture shows the image the user picked, currently limited to color options.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Avatar } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/avatar'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<Avatar type="initials" size="md" initials="BS" />
<Avatar type="host" size="lg" src={host.photoUrl} initials="BS" alt="Ben Shapiro" />
<Avatar type="show" size="sm" src={show.thumbnailUrl} alt={show.title} />
<Avatar type="dw-plus" size="2xsm" selected />
```

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.Default} />

<Controls of={AvatarStories.Default} />

The defaults (`type="initials"`, `size="4xsm"`) are the component's; Figma's default variant is `DW+ · 4xsm`.
Avatar renders a `<span>` and is not interactive. To make it act (open the account menu, switch profile), put it inside a `Button`, `IconButton` or link, which owns the focus ring and the touch target.

---

## Types

Figma's `Type` axis is the `type` prop:

- **DW+** (`dw-plus`) — the Daily Wire mark (`Brand / Logo › DailyWire Logo/Icon`) on a neutral disc. Ignores `src` and `initials`.
- **Initials** (`initials`) — one or two letters (Figma text property `Initials`, default "AA") on a purple disc.
- **Hosts** (`host`) — a host photo (Figma nests `Host Image`), circle.
- **People** (`person`) — a person photo (Figma nests `Person Image`), circle.
- **Shows** (`show`) — a show thumbnail (Figma nests `Shows Thumbnail`), **rounded square**.

Photos are drawn with `object-fit: cover`, centred, as Figma's `FILL` image fills.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.Types} />

---

## Sizes

Eleven sizes. The initials use the `label/400/*` text styles up to `md`, then `heading/400/*`:

| Size | px | Initials | Font size | DW+ logo width | Shows radius |
|------|----|----------|-----------|----------------|--------------|
| `4xsm` | 20 | `label/400/xs` | `--font-size-xxs` (10) | 14 | `--border-radius-sm` (4) |
| `3xsm` | 24 | `label/400/sm` | `--font-size-xs` (12) | 14 | `--border-radius-sm` (4) |
| `2xsm` | 32 | `label/400/md` | `--font-size-sm` (14) | 20 | `--border-radius-sm` (4) |
| `xsm` | 40 | `label/400/lg` | `--font-size-md` (16) | 24 | `--border-radius-md` (8) |
| `sm` | 48 | `label/400/xl` | `--font-size-lg` (20) | 32 | `--border-radius-md` (8) |
| `md` | 56 | `label/400/xl` | `--font-size-lg` (20) | 32 | `--border-radius-md` (8) |
| `lg` | 64 | `heading/400/md` | `--font-size-xl` (24) | 44 | `--border-radius-lg` (12) |
| `xl` | 72 | `heading/400/md` | `--font-size-xl` (24) | 44 | `--border-radius-lg` (12) |
| `2xl` | 80 | `heading/400/lg` | `--font-size-2xl` (32) | 52 | `--border-radius-lg` (12) |
| `3xl` | 96 | `heading/400/lg` | `--font-size-2xl` (32) | 52 (Figma 50) | `--border-radius-xl` (16) |
| `4xl` | 100 | `heading/400/lg` | `--font-size-2xl` (32) | 52 (Figma 54) | `--border-radius-xl` (16) |

Every other type is a full circle (`--border-radius-full`).

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.Sizes} />

---

## Selected

Figma `Selected=Yes` draws an **outside stroke with no gap** in `color/border/inverse/bold` (dark in Light, light in Dark).
The web component draws it as a 1px `outline` (`--border-default`) at every size; see the token notes below.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.Selected} />

`selected` is visual only. The control that holds the avatar must expose the state to assistive technology (`aria-pressed`, `aria-current`, `aria-selected`).

---

## Fallback

When an image type has no `src`, or the image fails to load, the avatar draws `initials` on the purple disc. Without initials it draws the Daily Wire mark.
`type="initials"` with empty `initials` also falls back to the mark. A new `src` gets a fresh attempt.
The element exposes what it draws as `data-content="image" | "initials" | "mark"`.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.Fallback} />

---

## Inspect

Pick type, size, selected and (for image types) a sample or missing image. The avatar is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Avatar is a static display element: Figma draws **no hover, pressed, focus or disabled** states, only `Selected`, so no others are implemented.
This grid shows every type and the image fallbacks × Selected No / Yes. Use the `size` control to switch sizes.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.States} />

The full Figma set, 110 variants: Size rows × Type, with Selected No on the left and Yes on the right, as in Figma.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.FigmaMatrix} />

## Image sources

The image types nest image-swap sets in Figma. They are **placeholders, not UI**: in the product, `src` is the real host photo, profile picture or show artwork.
For these docs each variant was exported at 200 × 200 px (2× the largest avatar, `4xl`), with its Figma crop, into `components/avatar/samples/`.
`components/avatar/samples/index.ts` exports them as `HOST_IMAGES`, `PERSON_IMAGES` and `SHOW_THUMBNAILS` (Figma variant name → URL) for stories and docs **only**: they are sample content, not package API, and are not exported from `components/avatar`.

### Host Image

Figma `Host Image` (10:1794), property `Background`, 12 variants. Used by `type="host"`.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.HostImages} />

### Person Image

Figma `Person Image` (8484:27270), property `Person`, 10 variants. Used by `type="person"`.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.PersonImages} />

### Shows Thumbnail

Figma `Shows Thumbnail` (12354:203323), property `Show`, 9 variants. Used by `type="show"`.

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.ShowThumbnails} />

---

## Accessibility

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| Semantics | `<span>`; with `alt` it is `role="img"` + `aria-label`, so one name is announced whatever is drawn (photo, initials or mark) |
| Decorative use | Without `alt` nothing is announced (inner image `alt=""`, initials and mark `aria-hidden`). Use this when a visible name sits next to the avatar |
| Selected | Visual only; the parent control exposes the state |
| Interaction | None: not focusable, no touch target of its own. Wrap it in a `Button` / `IconButton` / link to make it act |
| Contrast | Initials `--color-text-always-light` on the purple disc; the mark uses `--color-icon-neutral-bold-default` on `--color-background-neutral-subtlest-default` |

---

## Tokens

Every property of `Avatar.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

<Canvas of={AvatarStories.Tokens} />

**Token notes (Figma values without a token):**

- Initials disc: Figma raw `#774DB2` (no variable). Uses `--primitive-color-purple-600` (`#7c3aed`), the closest purple in lightness that keeps AA contrast with the light initials.
- Photo placeholder (Hosts / People / Shows): Figma raw `#292929` (no variable). Uses `--color-background-neutral-subtlest-default`, the variable DW+ binds for the same color.
- Selected ring: Figma stroke 0.5 (`4xsm`), 0.75 (`3xsm`–`xl`) and 1px (`2xl`–`4xl`), not bound. There is no sub-pixel border token, so `--border-default` (1px) is used at every size.
- Size `xl` 72, `2xl` 80, `3xl` 96: no `--size-*` token, `--primitive-dimension-dimension-18 · 20 · 24` used. Size `4xl` 100: no token, composed as `--primitive-dimension-dimension-24 + --primitive-dimension-dimension-1`.
- DW+ logo widths 14 / 44 / 52: primitive dimensions. Figma draws 50px at `3xl` and 54px at `4xl` (no token): 52px is used for both.

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={AvatarStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `type` | `'dw-plus' \| 'initials' \| 'host' \| 'person' \| 'show'` | `'initials'` | Figma `Type` |
| `size` | `'4xsm' \| '3xsm' \| '2xsm' \| 'xsm' \| 'sm' \| 'md' \| 'lg' \| 'xl' \| '2xl' \| '3xl' \| '4xl'` | `'4xsm'` | 20 → 100 px |
| `selected` | `boolean` | `false` | Figma `Selected`: outside ring |
| `src` | `string` | — | Image for `host`, `person`, `show` |
| `alt` | `string` | — | Accessible name; omit for a decorative avatar |
| `initials` | `string` | — | Figma `Initials`; also the image fallback |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<span>` attributes via spread (`id`, `title`, `aria-*`, etc.).
> Exports: `Avatar`, `AVATAR_TYPES`, `AVATAR_SIZES`, `AvatarProps`, `AvatarType`, `AvatarSize`.

States story ok
Badge is static: Figma draws no hover / pressed / focus / disabled. Every style × radius (rows) × weight (column groups), each with dot / icon off and on.
const States = ({ size = 'md', children }) => (
  <div style={{ padding: 24, overflowX: 'auto' }}>
    <div
      style={{
        display: 'grid',
        gridTemplateColumns: `auto repeat(${BADGE_WEIGHTS.length * SLOTS.length}, auto)`,
        gap: '16px 12px',
        alignItems: 'center',
        justifyItems: 'start',
        ...muted,
      }}
    >
      <div />
      {BADGE_WEIGHTS.map((w) => (
        <div key={w} style={{ gridColumn: `span ${SLOTS.length}`, textTransform: 'capitalize', fontWeight: 600 }}>{w}</div>
      ))}
      <div />
      {BADGE_WEIGHTS.flatMap((w) => SLOTS.map((s) => <div key={`${w}-${s}`}>{s}</div>))}
      {BADGE_VARIANTS.flatMap((variant) =>
        BADGE_RADII.map((radius) => (
          <Row key={`${variant}-${radius}`}>
            <div style={{ whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }}>{`${label(variant)} · radius ${radius}`}</div>
            {BADGE_WEIGHTS.flatMap((weight) =>
              SLOTS.map((slot) =>
                size === 'xsm' && radius === 'sm' ? (
                  <div key={`${weight}-${slot}`}>not in Figma</div>
                ) : (
                  <Badge key={`${weight}-${slot}`} variant={variant} size={size} weight={weight} radius={radius} {...slotProps(slot)}>
                    {children}
                  </Badge>
                ),
              ),
            )}
          </Row>
        )),
      )}
    </div>
  </div>
);

Docs doc ok
./components/badge/Badge.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes, Controls } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as BadgeStories from './Badge.stories'

<Meta of={BadgeStories} />

# Badge

A compact, non-interactive label that surfaces content metadata, status or editorial categorisation on thumbnails, cards and headers.
It has four visual styles, three sizes, three font weights, two corner radii, an optional status dot and an optional leading icon.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Badge` (6:10067, doc frame 2:12332, page "Badges, Labels, Filters, Chips, Tags").
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Badge } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/badge'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<Badge>New</Badge>
<Badge variant="translucid" size="md" weight="semibold" radius="sm" statusDot>Live</Badge>
<Badge variant="subtle" size="sm" leadingIcon="calendar-up">Coming soon</Badge>
```

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.Default} />

<Controls of={BadgeStories.Default} />

The defaults (`variant="brand"`, `size="xsm"`, `weight="regular"`, `radius="none"`) match the Figma component defaults.
Badge renders a `<span>`: it has no interaction. If a badge needs to act, put it inside the interactive parent (card, link, button).

---

## Variants

Four styles (Figma `style`):

- **Brand** — brand red surface, light label. Editorial highlights.
- **Inverse** — light surface, dark label, in both themes.
- **Translucid** — dark glass (`--color-overlay-neutral-strong-default` + background blur), light label, in both themes. Made to sit on imagery.
- **Subtle** — neutral fill that follows the theme.

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

Three sizes; the height hugs the label: **xsm** 18px (`label/*/xs`, 10px), **sm** 20px (`label/*/sm`, 12px), **md** 26px (`label/*/md`, 14px).
With a status dot or an icon (16px each) the badge grows to 24 / 24 / 28px, as the Figma auto layout does.

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.Sizes} />

---

## Weights

`regular` (400), `medium` (500) and `semibold` (600), the `label/400 · 500 · 600` text styles.

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.Weights} />

---

## Border radius

`none` (square) or `sm` (`--border-radius-sm`, 4px). Figma draws `sm` only for sizes **sm** and **md**; `size="xsm"` always renders square (a dev warning is logged if `radius="sm"` is passed).

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.Radius} />

---

## Status dot and icon

**Status dot** (Figma `Show Status Dot`, `StatusDot / live / md`): a 16px box with an 8px dot, before the label. It is decorative (`aria-hidden`), so the label must say what it means ("Live", "New").

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.WithStatusDot} />

**Leading icon** (Figma `Show left icon`, `utility / Size=sm`): a 16px glyph after the dot and before the label. The Figma default glyph is `calendar-up`; any catalog icon works.

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.WithIcon} />

> Figma binds the icon (and the brand dot) to theme-flipping icon tokens that lose contrast in Light mode. The web component pairs them with the label color instead: always-light on brand and translucid, always-dark on inverse, neutral-bold on subtle. In Dark mode the result equals the Figma rendering.

---

## Inspect

Pick style, size, weight, radius, status dot and icon. The badge is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Badge is a static display element: Figma draws **no hover, pressed, focus or disabled** states, so none are implemented.
This grid shows every style × radius × weight, each with the status dot and icon off and on. Use the `size` control to switch sizes.

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.States} />

---

## Full matrix

Every size × style × weight (radius none), as laid out in the Figma component set:

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.FullMatrix} />

---

## Accessibility

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| Semantics | Plain `<span>` with visible text; not focusable, no role |
| Status dot | `aria-hidden="true"`; meaning must be in the label |
| Icon | `aria-hidden="true"` (decorative) |
| Contrast | Label and icon colors come from paired text / icon tokens on each surface |
| Zoom | No fixed height: padding + `--line-height-100` let the badge grow with the text |

---

## Tokens

Every property of `Badge.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

<Canvas of={BadgeStories.Tokens} />

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={BadgeStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `'brand' \| 'inverse' \| 'translucid' \| 'subtle'` | `'brand'` | Visual style (Figma `style`) |
| `size` | `'xsm' \| 'sm' \| 'md'` | `'xsm'` | 18 / 20 / 26 px tall |
| `weight` | `'regular' \| 'medium' \| 'semibold'` | `'regular'` | Label font weight |
| `radius` | `'none' \| 'sm'` | `'none'` | Corner radius; `xsm` is always `none` |
| `statusDot` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows the 8px status dot before the label |
| `leadingIcon` | `IconName` | — | 16px icon before the label |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Label text |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<span>` attributes via spread (`id`, `title`, `aria-*`, etc.).

Variant Size Matrix story ok 
const VariantSizeMatrix = () => {
  const states = ['default', 'disabled', 'loading'] as const
  return (
    <div
      style={{
        display: 'grid',
        gridTemplateColumns: 'auto repeat(3, 1fr)',
        gap: '24px 16px',
        padding: 24,
        alignItems: 'center',
        fontSize: 12,
        color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)',
      }}
    >
      <div />
      {states.map((s) => (
        <div key={s} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
      ))}
      {BUTTON_VARIANTS.map((variant) =>
        BUTTON_SIZES.map((size, sizeIdx) => (
          <Row key={`${variant}-${size}`}>
            {sizeIdx === 0 && (
              <div style={{ gridRow: `span ${BUTTON_SIZES.length}`, alignSelf: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>
                {variant}
              </div>
            )}
            <Cell><Button variant={variant} size={size} label={size.toUpperCase()} leadingIcon="plus" /></Cell>
            <Cell><Button variant={variant} size={size} label={size.toUpperCase()} leadingIcon="plus" disabled /></Cell>
            <Cell><Button variant={variant} size={size} label={size.toUpperCase()} leadingIcon="plus" isLoading /></Cell>
          </Row>
        )),
      )}
    </div>
  )
};

}`}
      android={`import com.dailywire.ds.DWButton
import com.dailywire.ds.DWButtonVariant
import com.dailywire.ds.DWButtonSize

DWButton(
  label = "Subscribe",
  variant = DWButtonVariant.Primary,
  size = DWButtonSize.Medium,
  onClick = { /* action */ },
)`}
    />
  </div>
);

Every variant in every state. Hover / pressed / focus are forced with `data-state`.
const States = ({ size, leadingIcon }) => (
  <div style={{ display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: `auto repeat(${STATES.length}, 1fr)`, gap: '20px 12px', padding: 24, alignItems: 'center', fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)' }}>
    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
    ))}
    {BUTTON_VARIANTS.map((variant) => (
      <Row key={variant}>
        <div style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{variant}</div>
        {STATES.map((s) => (
          <Cell key={s}>
            <Button
              variant={variant}
              size={size}
              leadingIcon={leadingIcon}
              label={variant}
              disabled={s === 'disabled'}
              isLoading={s === 'loading'}
              {...(['hover', 'pressed', 'focus'].includes(s) ? { 'data-state': s } : {})}
            />
          </Cell>
        ))}
      </Row>
    ))}
  </div>
);

Docs doc ok
./components/button/Button.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as ButtonStories from './Button.stories'

<Meta of={ButtonStories} />

# Button

Daily Wire Design System button with six visual variants, three sizes, and support for icons, loading, and disabled states.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Button` (7387:18485) — link is its own component (`Link`).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Button } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/button'
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.Default} />

---

## Variants

Six visual variants:

- **Primary** — highest contrast (inverse surface).
- **Secondary** — outline.
- **Tertiary** — neutral fill.
- **Ghost** — text only.
- **Danger** — destructive actions.
- **Brand** — brand red, for brand moments; not the default call to action.

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

Three sizes with matching text styles: **sm** 32px (`label/600/sm`), **md** 48px (`label/600/md`), **lg** 56px (`label/600/lg`).
Includes rows with leading icons and icon-only examples.

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.Sizes} />

---

## With Icons

Leading icon next to the label; works with the full icon catalog.

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.WithIcon} />

### Icon only

No label—icon only. The button becomes square.

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.WithIconOnly} />

---

## Loading

Figma `Loader / Dots`: three 8px dots pulse in sequence (280ms, ease-in-out). The label is hidden visually and kept for screen readers.

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.WithLoading} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus shows the same ring on every variant: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset`.

---

## Inspect

Pick variant, state, size and icons. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every variant in every state (hover, pressed and focus are forced for review):

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.States} />

---

## Disabled

All variants in the disabled state.

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.IsDisabled} />

---

## Full matrix

All combinations of variant × size × state:

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.VariantSizeMatrix} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `Button.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

<Canvas of={ButtonStories.Tokens} />

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={ButtonStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `'primary' \| 'secondary' \| 'tertiary' \| 'ghost' \| 'danger' \| 'brand'` | `'primary'` | Visual variant |
| `size` | `'sm' \| 'md' \| 'lg'` | `'md'` | Button size (32/48/56 px) |
| `label` | `string` | `'Button'` | Button label text |
| `showLabel` | `boolean` | `true` | Show or hide the label (icon-only when `false`) |
| `leadingIcon` | `IconName` | — | Icon before the label |
| `trailingIcon` | `IconName` | — | Icon after the label |
| `isLoading` | `boolean` | `false` | Replaces content with the dots loader |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables the button |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

Docs doc ok
./components/byline/Byline.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as BylineStories from './Byline.stories'

<Meta of={BylineStories} />

# Byline

The author of an article: the name as a link, with an optional avatar before it. It is built from two DS components, `Avatar` and `Link`.
Two styles, two sizes, an avatar on or off, and four states (default, hover, focus, disabled).
Spec from Figma `Byline` (8210:45616, doc frame 8413:61617), section "Article" on page "↳ Content".
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Byline } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/byline'

<Byline
  author="Ben Shapiro"
  href="/authors/ben-shapiro"
  showAvatar
  avatar={{ type: 'host', src: benShapiroPhoto }}
/>
```

With `href` it renders the DS `Link` (a native `<a>`) around the avatar and the name, so hover, focus and the hit area cover both. The name is the accessible name; the avatar is decorative.
Without `href` it renders a `<span>` with the same look and no interaction — use that inside a card that is already one link (a link cannot contain another link).

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={BylineStories.Default} />

---

## Styles

Figma's `style` axis is the `variant` prop, passed to the nested `Link`:

- **Secondary** (default) — `text/neutral/subtlest` tokens.
- **Primary** — `text/neutral/bold` tokens.

<Canvas of={BylineStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

| Size | Text style | Font size | Avatar | Height |
|------|-----------|-----------|--------|--------|
| `sm` | `label/400/sm` | `--font-size-xs` (12) | `2xsm` (32) | 12 · 32 with avatar |
| `md` | `label/400/md` | `--font-size-sm` (14) | `2xsm` (32) | 14 · 32 with avatar |

The avatar sits `--spacing-sm` (6px) before the name, vertically centered.

<Canvas of={BylineStories.Sizes} />

---

## Avatar

Figma's `Show Avatar` boolean is `showAvatar` (off by default). The `avatar` prop carries the data: `type` (`host` by default, as in Figma, or `person`, `initials`), `src` and `initials`.
Without `initials`, they are derived from the author name ("Georgia Howe" → "GH"), and they also stand in when the photo fails to load.

<Canvas of={BylineStories.WithAvatar} />

---

## Static (no link)

<Canvas of={BylineStories.Static} />

---

## Hover and focus

Hover underlines the name and switches it to the `hovered` token. Keyboard focus draws the `Link` ring — `--focus-ring-width` in `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-offset` gap, `--border-radius-sm` corners — around the avatar and the name, and the name switches to `text-neutral-subtlest-selected`.

On touch screens the hit area grows to `--touch-target-min` without changing the layout.

---

## Disabled

A disabled byline renders `aria-disabled="true"` and `role="link"`, drops its `href` (so it cannot navigate or take focus) and ignores clicks.

<Canvas of={BylineStories.IsDisabled} />

---

## Inspect

Pick style, state, size, avatar and link mode. The byline is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state (with a link, the nested `Link` rules are included). Hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={BylineStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every style × avatar in every Figma state (hover and focus are forced for review):

<Canvas of={BylineStories.States} />

| State | Secondary | Primary |
|---|---|---|
| Default | `text-neutral-subtlest-default` | `text-neutral-bold-default` |
| Hover (+ underline) | `text-neutral-subtlest-hovered` | `text-neutral-bold-hovered` |
| Focus | `text-neutral-subtlest-selected` | `text-neutral-subtlest-selected` |
| Disabled | `text-neutral-subtlest-disabled` | `text-neutral-bold-disabled` |

---

All 16 Figma variants (state × size × style), with the avatar off and on.

<Canvas of={BylineStories.FigmaMatrix} />

Every property of `Byline.module.css`, then the nested `Link.module.css` that colors the linked byline, with the token each uses resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={BylineStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `author` | `string` | — | Author name; the visible text and the accessible name |
| `href` | `string` | — | Author page. Omit for static text |
| `variant` | `'secondary' \| 'primary'` | `'secondary'` | Figma `style` |
| `size` | `'sm' \| 'md'` | `'sm'` | 12 or 14 px name |
| `showAvatar` | `boolean` | `false` | 32px avatar before the name |
| `avatar` | `{ src?: string; type?: 'host' \| 'person' \| 'initials'; initials?: string }` | — | Avatar data |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | `aria-disabled`, no `href`, clicks blocked |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class on the root |

> Also accepts native `<a>` attributes via spread (`target`, `rel`, `onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

CardFeed

components-cardfeed · ./components/card-feed/CardFeed.stories.tsx
Feed item for articles, episodes, live blogs and shorts. Accessible card pattern: the title is the only link (ArticleHeading `stretched`) and covers the card; the more button, a linked byline and the shorts video sit above it as separate controls. Desktop / Mobile follow the card's own width (container query at 480px).
Prop types (react-docgen) 22 prop types 
Component: components/card-feed/CardFeed.tsx::CardFeed
Props:
/**
 * Byline name (Figma Byline · primary). Show name for `episode`, outlet for `live-blog`.
 */
author?: string

/**
 * Author / show page. Omit to keep the byline static text, so it belongs to the card link.
 */
authorHref?: string

/**
 * Leading avatar (48px Desktop, 32px Mobile). Decorative: the byline carries the name.
 */
avatar?: CardFeedAvatar

/**
 * Figma `Content`: the kind of item (see CARD_FEED_CONTENTS). Sets the defaults of every text below.
 */
content?: (typeof CARD_FEED_CONTENTS)[number] = 'article-free'

/**
 * Relative or absolute date, shown as is ("3 min ago")
 */
date?: string = '3 min ago'

/**
 * Machine-readable date (ISO 8601). When set the date renders as `<time dateTime>`.
 */
dateTime?: string

/**
 * Figma `Description` (body/400/sm, 3 lines max). Default only for article-free, article-premium and live-blog. Empty string hides it.
 */
description?: string

/**
 * Episode length, on the thumbnail badge (Figma "1h 20m"). `episode` only.
 */
duration?: string = '1h 20m'

/**
 * Screen-reader prefix of the duration badge
 */
durationLabel?: string = 'Duration'

/**
 * Heading element of the title
 */
headingLevel?: 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 = 3

/**
 * Destination of the card. The title is the card's only link and its hit area covers the whole card.
 */
href: string

/**
 * Alt text of the picture. Default `''` (decorative: the title names the card).
 */
imageAlt?: string = ''

/**
 * Picture of article / live-blog cards and episode thumbnail (16:9, cover). Thumbnail is outside the Article section: a plain `<img>`.
 */
imageSrc?: string

/**
 * Forces a Figma `InteractionState` for docs and QA. Real hover / press / focus work without it.
 */
interactionState?: (typeof CARD_FEED_INTERACTION_STATES)[number] = 'default'

/**
 * Kind label after the byline ("Article", "Opinion", "Ep. 1520", "Live blog", "Short")
 */
label?: string

/**
 * Extra attributes for the title `<a>` (`target`, `rel`, `onClick`…)
 */
linkProps?: ArticleHeadingProps['linkProps']

/**
 * Extra props for the more button (`aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`…)
 */
moreButtonProps?: Omit<IconButtonProps, 'icon' | 'type' | 'size' | 'aria-label' | 'onClick'>

/**
 * Accessible name of the more button
 */
moreLabel?: string = 'More options'

/**
 * Called by the more button. It sits outside the card link.
 */

/**
 * Figma Icon Button (ghost · dots-vertical) top-right
 */

/**
 * Figma `Body Text` (heading/500/md; serif for `article-premium`)

title?: string

/**
 * `shorts`: props of the 9:16 `ArticleVideo` (src, poster, autoPlay, muted…). `label` defaults to the title.
 */

import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as CardFeedStories from './CardFeed.stories'
