# CardFeed

A feed item for articles, episodes, live blogs and shorts: avatar, byline row with a more button, title, optional description and media.
The whole card is one link (the title), with the more button, a linked byline and the shorts video as separate controls on top.
Spec from Figma `Card Feed` (12354:203104, doc frame 11904:206370), section "Article" (8413:56868) on page "↳ Content":
`Content` Article Free | Article Premium | Episode | Live Blog | Shorts × `Breakpoint` Desktop | Mobile × `InteractionState` default | hovered | pressed (30 variants).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { CardFeed } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/card-feed'

<CardFeed
  content="article-free"
  href="/news/three-counties"
  author="Jennie Tyler"
  label="Article"
  date="3 min ago"
  dateTime="2026-11-05T14:57:00Z"
  title="Three Counties That Decide Pennsylvania"
  description="The FBI recently warned California authorities…"
  imageSrc="/media/three-counties.jpg"
  onMoreClick={openMenu}
/>
```

Built from DS parts: `Avatar`, `Byline` (primary), `IconButton` (ghost · `dots-vertical`), `ArticleHeading` (the stretched title link), `Badge` (translucid, episode duration) and `ArticleVideo` (shorts).
Figma's Thumbnail and Episode thumb live outside the Article section, so the picture is a plain `<img>` (`imageSrc` / `imageAlt`).

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.Default} />

---

## Content

Figma's `Content` is the `content` prop. It sets the default texts and avatar type of each kind, and what the card draws:

| `content` | Avatar | Label | Title | Description | Media |
|-----------|--------|-------|-------|-------------|-------|
| `article-free` | `initials` | "Article" | Libre Franklin | yes | 16:9 picture |
| `article-premium` | `host` | "Opinion" | **Libre Caslon** (`ArticleHeading tone="exclusive"`) | yes | 16:9 picture |
| `episode` | `show` (rounded square) | "Ep. 1520" | Libre Franklin | — | 16:9 thumbnail + duration `Badge` |
| `live-blog` | `dw-plus` | brand dot + "Live blog" | Libre Franklin | yes | 16:9 picture |
| `shorts` | `host` | "Short" | Libre Franklin | — | 9:16 `ArticleVideo` |

Every text is a prop with the Figma text as default (`author`, `label`, `date`, `title`, `description`, `duration`). An empty `description` hides it.
Figma draws no lock or premium badge on Article Premium: the serif title is the only difference.

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.Contents} />

---

## Breakpoints

Figma's `Breakpoint` axis is **not a prop**. The card is a size container and switches on its own width (`tokens.css` has no breakpoint tokens, so the limit sits between the Figma frames, 608 and 361):

| Breakpoint | Card width | Avatar | Byline row | Title | More button | Column gap | Shorts video |
|------------|------------|--------|------------|-------|-------------|------------|--------------|
| Desktop | ≥ 480 | `sm` 48 | label/400/md (14) | heading/500/md (24) | ghost · `md` | `--spacing-4xl` | 3/5 of the column |
| Mobile | < 480 | `2xsm` 32 | label/400/sm (12) | heading/500/xs (16) | ghost · `xsm` | `--spacing-3xl` | 2/3 of the column |

The episode badge goes from `md` to `sm` and the live dot from 8 to 6px.

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Card link and secondary actions

The title is the card's only link: `ArticleHeading` with `stretched`, so its hit area covers the whole card and hovering anywhere on it recolors the title.
Controls that do something else sit **outside** the `<a>` and above it:

- the more button (`onMoreClick`, `moreLabel`, `moreButtonProps` for `aria-haspopup` / `aria-expanded`);
- the byline, when `authorHref` is set (a `Link`); without it the byline is plain text and clicks go to the card;
- the shorts video (mute and play / pause buttons, click to play).

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.SecondaryActions} />

### In a feed

The card has no outer spacing: the page sets the gap between items.

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.Feed} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus lands on the title link and draws `--focus-ring-width` in `--color-focus-info-bold-default` around the **whole card** (`--focus-ring-offset`, `--border-radius-md` corners), since the link covers it. Figma has no focused variant; `interactionState="focused"` shows it in the docs.

---

## Inspect

Pick Content, Breakpoint and InteractionState. The card is drawn at the Figma frame width with its real measurements; the panel lists the card tokens that apply, plus the title rules from `ArticleHeading` (which owns the hovered / pressed colors).

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

All 30 Figma variants (plus the focus ring), Desktop then Mobile. `InteractionState` only recolors the title: `--color-text-neutral-bold-default` → `-hovered` → `-pressed`.

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `CardFeed.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme: Desktop (base) first, then the Mobile container query. `Avatar`, `Byline`, `IconButton`, `ArticleHeading`, `Badge` and `ArticleVideo` document their own tokens.

<Canvas of={CardFeedStories.Tokens} />

---

## Accessibility

- The root is an `<article>` named by its title (`aria-labelledby`). `headingLevel` sets the title element (default `h3`).
- One link per card (the title); no interactive element is nested in it. The avatar, label, date, description and picture belong to the link's hit area.
- The more button is an `IconButton` named by `moreLabel` ("More options"); pass `moreButtonProps` for menu semantics. Its hit area grows to `--touch-target-min` on touch screens.
- The picture is decorative by default (`imageAlt=""`): the title names the card. Set `imageAlt` when the picture adds information.
- The live dot is decorative; "Live blog" carries the meaning. The duration badge has a screen-reader prefix (`durationLabel`, "Duration").
- `dateTime` renders the date as `<time dateTime>`.
- Avatar, more button and duration badge exist once per breakpoint; the other one is `display: none`, so it is neither visible nor announced.
- The title color transition stops under `prefers-reduced-motion` (ArticleHeading).

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={CardFeedStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `content` | `'article-free' \| 'article-premium' \| 'episode' \| 'live-blog' \| 'shorts'` | `'article-free'` | Figma `Content`; sets defaults and media |
| `href` | `string` | — | Card destination (the title link) |
| `linkProps` | `AnchorHTMLAttributes` | — | Extra attributes for the title `<a>` (`target`, `rel`, `onClick`…) |
| `title` | `string` | Figma text | Card title |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5 \| 6` | `3` | Title element |
| `description` | `string` | Figma text (article / live blog) | 3 lines max; `''` hides it |
| `author` · `authorHref` | `string` | Figma text · — | Byline; with `authorHref` it is a link outside the card link |
| `avatar` | `{ type?, src?, initials? }` | per `content` | Leading avatar (decorative); initials fall back to the author's |
| `label` | `string` | Figma text | Kind label after the byline |
| `date` · `dateTime` | `string` | `'3 min ago'` · — | Date text; `dateTime` renders `<time>` |
| `imageSrc` · `imageAlt` | `string` | — · `''` | 16:9 picture (all but `shorts`) |
| `duration` · `durationLabel` | `string` | `'1h 20m'` · `'Duration'` | Episode badge |
| `video` | `ArticleVideoProps` (no `aspect`) | — | Shorts video (`src`, `poster`, `autoPlay`, `muted`…); `label` defaults to the title |
| `showMore` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma Icon Button |
| `moreLabel` · `onMoreClick` · `moreButtonProps` | — | `'More options'` | More button |
| `interactionState` | `'default' \| 'hovered' \| 'pressed' \| 'focused'` | `'default'` | Forces a state for docs / QA |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class on the root |

> `ref` points at the root `<article>`. Other `<article>` attributes are forwarded.

CardFeedAd

components-cardfeedad · ./components/card-feed-ad/CardFeedAd.stories.tsx
Sponsored feed item (image, video or product carousel). Accessible card pattern: the headline is the link and covers the card; the sponsorship label names the card and describes the link. The video controls, an interactive CTA and the carousel (scrollable, one link per product) sit above the card link.

 * Advertiser avatar (48px Desktop, 32px Mobile). Decorative.

Docs doc ok
./components/card-feed-ad/CardFeedAd.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as CardFeedAdStories from './CardFeedAd.stories'

<Meta of={CardFeedAdStories} />

# CardFeedAd

A sponsored item for the feed: advertiser avatar, an "Advertisement" label, headline, description, then a picture, a video or a product carousel with a caption and a call to action.
The whole card is one link (the headline) to the advertiser; the video controls and the carousel products are separate controls on top.
Spec from Figma `Card Feed Ad` (12432:14077, doc frame 12447:22595), section "Article" (8413:56868) on page "↳ Content":
`Media` Image | Video | Carousel × `Ratio` 1x1 | 16x9 | 9x16 × `Breakpoint` Desktop | Mobile × `InteractionState` default | hovered | pressed (54 variants),
plus `Show caption`, `Show description`, `Show caption subtitle`, `Show CTA`, `Headline`, `Description`, `Caption title` and `Caption subtitle`.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { CardFeedAd } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/card-feed-ad'

<CardFeedAd
  media="image"
  ratio="16/9"
  href="https://advertiser.example/pumpkin-spice"
  linkProps={{ target: '_blank', onClick: trackAdClick }}
  headline="PUMPKIN SPICE"
  description="The season's here and so is the coffee to match…"
  imageSrc="/ads/pumpkin-spice.jpg"
  captionTitle="PUMPKIN SPICE"
  captionSubtitle="$26.95"
  ctaLabel="GET"
/>
```

Built from DS parts: `Avatar`, `ArticleHeading` (the stretched headline link), `ArticleVideo` (`media="video"`, with the `aspect` of `ratio`), `Button` (secondary · sm CTA) and `Icon` (`star-filled` / `star-half` / `star-outline`).
Figma's Thumbnail lives outside the Article section, so pictures are plain `<img>` elements.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.Default} />

---

## Media

Figma's `Media` is the `media` prop:

- `image` — `imageSrc` / `imageAlt` in a bordered shell, caption bar below.
- `video` — an `ArticleVideo` (`video` props: `src`, `poster`, `autoPlay`, `muted`…) in the same shell. Its mute and play / pause buttons are its own controls; clicking the picture plays / pauses instead of opening the ad.
- `carousel` — a horizontal list of products (`items`), each with picture, name, stars, reviews and price, and no CTA.

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.Media} />

### Why a private carousel (not `LiveUpdatesSlider`)

Figma's carousel is a scrolling **track** that shows two-and-a-bit products at once, with no arrows, dots or "n of m" slides; it bleeds to the card's left edge while scrolling.
`LiveUpdatesSlider` is a one-slide-at-a-time slider with arrows, dots and slide semantics. So the carousel is a private scroll-snap list (`<ul aria-label="Products">`):
swipe, trackpad or Shift + wheel to scroll (a thin scrollbar shows for mouse users), and every product is a link that keyboard users reach with Tab (the track scrolls it into view).
Each product links to `items[].href`, or to the card `href`.

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.Carousel} />

---

## Ratio

Figma's `Ratio` (`16/9`, `1/1`, `9/16`) shapes the picture, the video or each carousel picture (cover). A `9/16` picture or video takes 3/5 of the column (Figma 332 of 548px; no width token).

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.Ratios} />

---

## Breakpoints

Figma's `Breakpoint` axis is **not a prop**. The card is a size container and switches on its own width (`tokens.css` has no breakpoint tokens, so the limit sits between the Figma frames, 608 and 361):

| Breakpoint | Card width | Avatar | Headline | Media indent | Carousel product |
|------------|------------|--------|----------|--------------|------------------|
| Desktop | ≥ 480 | `sm` 48 | heading/500/md (24) | `--size-3xl` + `--spacing-lg` | 43 % of the track |
| Mobile | < 480 | `2xsm` 32 | heading/500/xs (16) | `--size-xl` + `--spacing-lg` | 50 % of the track |

On Mobile, the narrow `9/16` shell stacks its caption and the CTA takes the full width.

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Caption options

The Figma boolean props map 1:1: `showDescription`, `showCaption` (the caption bar, or each product's text), `showCaptionSubtitle` (price) and `showCta`.
The 9x16 variants label the CTA "SHOP" (`ctaLabel`).

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.CaptionOptions} />

### CTA

The CTA does what the card does, so by default it is drawn by `Button` but **inert**: a click on it is a click on the card link, it is not a second tab stop, and its label is part of the link's name ("PUMPKIN SPICE GET").
Pass `onCtaClick` when it does something else (e.g. add to cart): it becomes a real button above the card link.

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.InteractiveCta} />

### In a feed

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.InAFeed} />

---

## Sponsored labelling

- The label above the headline (`sponsoredLabel`, "Advertisement", drawn uppercase like Figma's "ADVERTISEMENT") is visible text, never an icon or color only.
- The root `<article>` is named "Advertisement PUMPKIN SPICE" (`aria-labelledby` = label + headline).
- The headline link is described by the label (`aria-describedby`), so a link list reads "PUMPKIN SPICE GET, Advertisement".
- Links get `rel="sponsored"` by default (override with `linkProps.rel`).

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus on the headline link draws `--focus-ring-width` in `--color-focus-info-bold-default` around the **whole card** (`--focus-ring-offset`, `--border-radius-md` corners). Carousel products draw the same ring around themselves. Figma has no focused variant; `interactionState="focused"` shows it in the docs.

---

Pick Media, Ratio, Breakpoint and InteractionState. The card is drawn at the Figma frame width with its real measurements; the panel lists the card tokens that apply, plus the headline rules from `ArticleHeading` (which owns the hovered / pressed colors).

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

All 54 Figma variants (plus the focus ring), Desktop then Mobile. `InteractionState` only recolors the headline: `--color-text-neutral-bold-default` → `-hovered` → `-pressed`.

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `CardFeedAd.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme: Desktop (base) first, then the Mobile container query. `Avatar`, `ArticleHeading`, `ArticleVideo`, `Button` and `Icon` document their own tokens.

<Canvas of={CardFeedAdStories.Tokens} />

---

## Accessibility

- One card link (the headline); nothing interactive is nested in it. Avatar, label, description, picture and caption belong to its hit area.
- Pictures are decorative by default (`imageAlt=""`, `items[].alt=""`): the headline and product names describe them.
- Stars are decorative; a screen-reader sentence carries the rating (`ratingLabel`, "Rated 4 out of 5"). Half stars round to the nearest 0.5.
- Without a caption, each product link is named by its title (`aria-label`).
- The avatar exists once per breakpoint; the other one is `display: none`.
- Carousel smooth scrolling and the headline color transition stop under `prefers-reduced-motion`.

---

|------|------|---------|-------------|

| `ratio` | `'16/9' \| '1/1' \| '9/16'` | `'16/9'` | Figma `Ratio` |

| `avatar` | `{ type?, src?, initials? }` | `{ type: 'dw-plus' }` | Advertiser avatar (decorative) |
| `headline` · `headingLevel` | `string` · `2–6` | `'PUMPKIN SPICE'` · `3` | Figma `Headline` |
| `description` · `showDescription` | `string` · `boolean` | Figma text · `true` | Figma `Description` / `Show description` |
| `imageSrc` · `imageAlt` | `string` | — · `''` | `media="image"` |
| `video` | `ArticleVideoProps` (no `aspect`) | — | `media="video"`; `label` defaults to the headline |
| `items` · `itemsLabel` | `CardFeedAdItem[]` · `string` | 3 Figma slides · `'Products'` | `media="carousel"`: `{ src?, alt?, title?, rating?, reviewCount?, price?, href? }` |
| `showCaption` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show caption` |
| `captionTitle` · `captionSubtitle` · `showCaptionSubtitle` | `string` · `string` · `boolean` | `'PUMPKIN SPICE'` · `'$26.95'` · `true` | Caption texts (also the products' defaults) |
| `showCta` · `ctaLabel` · `onCtaClick` | `boolean` · `string` · handler | `true` · `'GET'` · — | CTA (image / video); inert unless `onCtaClick` |
| `ratingLabel` | `(rating) => string` | `Rated n out of 5` | Screen-reader text of the stars |
| `interactionState` | `'default' \| 'hovered' \| 'pressed' \| 'focused'` | `'default'` | Forces a state for docs / QA |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class on the root |

> `ref` points at the root `<article>`. Other `<article>` attributes are forwarded.

# Close Overlay Button

Round, icon-only button that closes an overlay (player, sheet, image viewer).
Its accessible name defaults to **"Close"**; pass a more specific `aria-label` when the page has several overlays (for example "Close player").
Spec reviewed against Figma `Close Overlay` (1649:2227).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

> **Legacy source.** Figma binds old variables: `Background/Container` (#151515) → `--color-background-neutral-subtle-default`;
> the `Close_24px` icon is `Icon/Strong` (white) at 30% opacity → `--color-icon-neutral-subtlest-default` (no opacity token);
> `radius-pill` → `--border-radius-full`. The shadow (4, 4, blur 16, black 40%) and background blur (90) have no token; it uses `--shadow-lg` and no blur.

Lifecycle story ok
Composition: a simulated download. Click to start, pause, resume; it completes on its own.
const Lifecycle = () => {
    const [state, setState] = useState<DownloadState>('default')
    const [progress, setProgress] = useState(0)
    useEffect(() => {
      if (state === 'starting') {
        const t = setTimeout(() => setState('ongoing'), 1200)
        return () => clearTimeout(t)
      }
      if (state === 'ongoing') {
        const t = setInterval(() => setProgress((p) => Math.min(1, p + 0.02)), 100)
        return () => clearInterval(t)
      }
    }, [state])
    useEffect(() => {
      if (progress >= 1) setState('completed')
    }, [progress])
    const next: Record<DownloadState, () => void> = {
      default: () => { setProgress(0); setState('starting') },
      starting: () => setState('default'),
      ongoing: () => setState('paused'),
      paused: () => setState('ongoing'),
      completed: () => { setProgress(0); setState('default') },
      expired: () => { setProgress(0); setState('starting') },
    }

    return (
        <ShowcaseBox direction="column">
            <DownloadButton
                disabled={false}
                size={args.size ?? 'md'}
                downloadState={state}
                progress={progress}
                onClick={next[state]} />
            <span style={caption}>{state}{state === 'ongoing' || state === 'paused' ? ` · ${Math.round(progress * 100)}%` : ''}</span>
        </ShowcaseBox>
    );
};

Every Figma variant: Download State × Interaction State (Type is always minimal). Hover / pressed / focus are forced with `data-state`. Figma only draws Disabled for Download State=default; the other disabled cells follow the code.

    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
    ))}
    {DOWNLOAD_STATES.map((ds) => (
      <Row key={ds}>
        <div style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{ds}</div>
        {STATES.map((s) => (
          <Cell key={s}>
            <DownloadButton
              size={size}
              downloadState={ds}
              progress={progress}
              disabled={s === 'disabled'}
              {...(['hover', 'pressed', 'focus'].includes(s) ? { 'data-state': s } : {})}
            />
          </Cell>
        ))}
      </Row>
    ))}
  </div>
);

./components/download-button/DownloadButton.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as DownloadButtonStories from './DownloadButton.stories'

<Meta of={DownloadButtonStories} />

# Download Button

A stateful control that communicates the full lifecycle of a content download — from idle to in progress, paused, completed and expired. It is used on episode and audio cards wherever offline playback is supported.

It has two independent state axes: the **interaction state** (hover, pressed, focus, disabled — user input) and the **download state** (business logic, the `downloadState` prop). The glyph inside the button is decided entirely by the download state.

Spec reviewed against Figma `Download Button` (8589:136144, doc frame 8577:138445): Type minimal × Size xsm / sm / md / lg × Interaction State × Download State (100 variants).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { DownloadButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/download-button'
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={DownloadButtonStories.Default} />

```tsx
<DownloadButton
  downloadState={download.state}   // 'default' | 'starting' | 'ongoing' | 'paused' | 'completed' | 'expired'
  progress={download.progress}     // 0–1, used by ongoing / paused
  onClick={handleDownloadAction}
/>
```

---

## Download states

| State | Glyph | Accessible name (default) | Color family |
|---|---|---|---|
| `default` | download arrow | Download | `icon/neutral/subtlest` |
| `starting` | dashed ring (turning) + stop square | Cancel download | `icon/neutral/bold` |
| `ongoing` | progress ring + stop square | Pause download | `icon/neutral/bold` |
| `paused` | progress ring + play | Resume download | `icon/neutral/bold` |
| `completed` | phone with check | Downloaded | `icon/neutral/subtlest` |
| `expired` | clock with exclamation | Download expired | `icon/warning/bold` |

The name always describes what the button does next; override any of them with `labels` (e.g. for localisation).

<Canvas of={DownloadButtonStories.DownloadStates} />

---

## Progress

In `ongoing` and `paused` the ring draws `progress` (0–1) clockwise from 12 o'clock: the track is `--color-icon-neutral-subtlest-default`, the arc `--color-icon-neutral-bold-default` — both stay the same in every interaction state, as in Figma. The ring is 1/13 of the glyph thick (Figma's *Circular progress bar*).
A `<button>` cannot carry `aria-valuenow`, so the percentage is exposed as the button's description (`aria-describedby`): a screen reader hears "Pause download, button, 45%".

<Canvas of={DownloadButtonStories.Progress} />

### Starting

Figma has no prototype reaction for the starting ring; code turns it once per second (linear) to read as "preparing". With `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the ring stays still.

---

## Sizes

| Size | Box | Padding | Glyph | Inner glyph (stop) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `xsm` (default) | 24 | `--spacing-xxs` | `--size-md` 20 | `--size-2xs` 8 |
| `sm` | 32 | `--spacing-xs` | `--size-lg` 24 | `--size-xs` 12 |
| `md` | 40 | `--spacing-md` | `--size-lg` 24 | `--size-xs` 12 |
| `lg` | 48 | `--spacing-lg` | `--size-lg` 24 | `--size-xs` 12 |

The paused play glyph is `--size-xs` (12) in every size. Sizes below 44px get an invisible hit area of `--touch-target-min` around them, so the visual size matches Figma while the touch target stays accessible.

<Canvas of={DownloadButtonStories.Sizes} />

---

## Lifecycle

A simulated download: click to start, pause and resume; it completes on its own.

<Canvas of={DownloadButtonStories.Lifecycle} />

---

## Focus

Figma draws the focus ring as a single 2px `--color-focus-info-bold-default` spread right at the edge of the round container (no gap): code uses `outline: var(--focus-ring-width)` with `outline-offset: var(--spacing-none)`.
Figma also fills the focused container with `background/neutral/bold` only as a workaround for the shadow (doc note); the container stays transparent in code.

---

# Follow Button

A text toggle to follow or unfollow a host. Not following shows **Follow**; following shows **Following**, dimmed, with an optional check before it.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Follow` (395:16301), docs frame “Follow - Unfollow” in Section 1 (13898:9752).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

The Figma set still uses the legacy `[Old]` variables (`Text/Default/Base`, `Text/Default/Strong`, `Icon/Strong`) and only draws the default state.
Here they map to `text/neutral/bold`, `text/neutral/subtlest` and `icon/neutral/subtlest`; hover, pressed and disabled use the same families, and focus uses the shared ring.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { FollowButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/follow-button'
```

---

## Usage

Click to toggle. Uncontrolled with `defaultSelected`, or controlled with `selected` + `onSelectedChange`:

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.Default} />

```tsx
const [following, setFollowing] = useState(false)

<FollowButton selected={following} onSelectedChange={setFollowing} />
```

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.Controlled} />

---

## Sizes

Both sizes use 14px (`font-size-sm`):

- **sm** (Figma Small) — sentence case, line height 150%. Follow is Semibold (`body/600/sm`), Following is Regular (`body/400/sm`).
- **lg** (Figma Big) — uppercase, Semibold (`label/600/md`).

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.Sizes} />

---

## Without check

Figma `Show Check` off.

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.WithoutCheck} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus shows the same ring as Button: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset`. The radius (`border-radius-sm`) only shapes the ring.

---

## Inspect

Pick following, state, size and check. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that combination — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Following? × Size in every state (hover, pressed and focus are forced for review):

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.States} />

---

## Disabled

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.IsDisabled} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `FollowButton.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar). Rows under “following” only apply while following:

<Canvas of={FollowButtonStories.Tokens} />

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={FollowButtonStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `size` | `'sm' \| 'lg'` | `'sm'` | Figma `Size`: Small / Big |
| `label` | `string` | `'Follow'` | Label while not following |
| `selectedLabel` | `string` | `'Following'` | Label while following |
| `showCheck` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Check` |
| `selected` | `boolean` | — | Controlled following state (`aria-pressed`) |
| `defaultSelected` | `boolean` | `false` | Initial state when uncontrolled |
| `onSelectedChange` | `(selected: boolean) => void` | — | Called with the next state on every toggle |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables the button |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.). Calling `event.preventDefault()` in `onClick` cancels the toggle.
> The accessible name is the visible label, so it reads “Follow” or “Following”, and `aria-pressed` reports the state.

# Header

The Daily Wire web header: a `<header>` landmark with the main navigation, the Daily Wire logo, and the account actions (Download App, Search, the account control or Log in / Sign up, and an optional upgrade promo).
Spec from Figma `Header (Web)` (7827:6984) on the "↳ Navigation" page: `loginState` × `breakpoint` × `Promo` (9 variants) plus the `Show logo` and `Show <section>` booleans.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Header, HEADER_NAV_ITEMS } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/header'

<Header
  loggedIn
  account={<MyAccountButton />}
  items={HEADER_NAV_ITEMS.map((item) => ({ ...item, selected: item.href === pathname }))}
  onSearchClick={openSearch}
  onAccountClick={openAccountMenu}
/>
```

Built from DS parts: `NavigationItem` (sections, Download App, Search), `Button` (Log in, Sign up, Upgrade), `IconButton` (menu, compact search, default account control) and the `Brand / Logo` artwork (`icons/brand/dailywire-logo.svg`).

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.Default} />

---

## Breakpoints

Figma's `breakpoint` axis is **not a prop**. The header switches layout with CSS container queries on its own width, so it follows the space it gets: full width in an app, a fixed frame in these docs.
`tokens.css` has no breakpoint tokens yet, so the limits are the Figma frame widths:

| Breakpoint | Header width | Padding (block · inline) | Start | End |
|------------|--------------|--------------------------|-------|-----|
| Mobile | < 768 | `--spacing-lg` · `--spacing-md` (logged out: `--spacing-xl` on the right) | menu button | compact search, account · or Sign up only |
| Tablet | 768 – 1439 | `--spacing-lg` · `--spacing-lg` left, `--spacing-2xl` right | menu button | compact search, account, promo · or Sign up only |
| Desktop | ≥ 1440 | `--spacing-xl` · `--spacing-8xl` | navigation | Download App, Search, divider, account, promo · or Log in + Sign up |

The bar is 64 px tall on every breakpoint (padding + 32 px items on desktop, + the 40 px menu button below). The logo stays centered.

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Login state

`loggedIn` maps to Figma `loginState`. Signed in: the account control. Figma nests an `Avatar` (size 4xsm: a photo on desktop and tablet, initials on mobile) inside an `Icon Button` minimal · sm. The DS has no Avatar component yet, so the `account` prop is a slot for your own button + avatar; without it the header renders a `user` `IconButton` (minimal · sm, named by `accountLabel`). Signed out: `Log in` (ghost, desktop only) and `Sign up` (brand); below desktop Figma shows Sign up alone, without the search button.

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.LoginState} />

---

## Promo

`promo` maps to Figma `Promo`: a brand `Button` after the account control (`promoLabel`, default "Upgrade"). It applies to signed-in users only (Figma has no logged-out promo variant) and is hidden on mobile, where Figma draws Promo=yes the same as Promo=no. On tablet a divider separates it from the account control.

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.Promo} />

---

## Mobile menu

Below desktop the navigation moves behind the menu button, a disclosure with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` pointing at the `<nav>`. Activating it shows the same `<nav aria-label="Main">` as a panel under the bar; Escape closes it and returns focus to the button. Use `menuOpen` + `onMenuOpenChange` to control it, or `defaultMenuOpen`.
The open panel is not drawn in Figma: it reuses the header surface and bottom stroke.

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.MobileMenu} />

---

## Sections and logo

The Figma `Show News`, `Show Shows & Movies`, `Show All Access`, `Show Shop`, `Show Kids` and `Show DWTV` booleans become the `items` array: `HEADER_NAV_ITEMS` holds the six sections in Figma order, filter or replace it. Mark the current section with `selected` (`aria-current="page"`, 600 weight). `showLogo` maps to `Show logo`; the logo is a link (`logoHref`, default `/`) whose accessible name is "The Daily Wire".

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.CustomItems} />

---

## Inspect

Pick login, promo and breakpoint. The header is drawn at the frame width (scaled to fit) with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply at that width, including the container-query rules.

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

All 9 Figma variants (login × promo at each breakpoint), plus a desktop row with navigation items forced to selected, hover, pressed and focus:

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `Header.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme: the shared rules first, then each breakpoint range. Nested components (`NavigationItem`, `Button`, `IconButton`) document their own tokens.

<Canvas of={HeaderStories.Tokens} />

---

## Accessibility

- `<header>` is the banner landmark; the sections live in `<nav aria-label="Main">` (`navLabel`) as a list.
- The menu button is a disclosure (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`), labelled by `menuLabel` ("Menu").
- Icon-only controls have names: "Search", "Account" (`accountLabel`). A custom `account` control must carry its own accessible name.
- Every control shows the DS focus ring; touch screens get `--touch-target-min` hit areas.

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={HeaderStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `loggedIn` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `loginState`: account button or Log in / Sign up |
| `promo` | `boolean` | `false` | Figma `Promo`: upgrade button (signed in, tablet and desktop) |
| `promoLabel` | `string` | `'Upgrade'` | Promo button label |
| `onPromoClick` | `MouseEventHandler` | — | Promo button click |
| `items` | `HeaderNavItem[]` | `HEADER_NAV_ITEMS` | `{ label, href, selected?, icon? }` plus any `NavigationItem` prop |
| `navLabel` | `string` | `'Main'` | Accessible name of the `<nav>` |
| `showLogo` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show logo` |
| `logoHref` | `string` | `'/'` | Logo link target |
| `showDownloadApp` | `boolean` | `true` | Desktop "Download App" item |
| `downloadAppHref` | `string` | — | Renders Download App as a link |
| `onDownloadAppClick` | `MouseEventHandler` | — | Download App click |
| `showSearch` | `boolean` | `true` | Search item (desktop) / icon button (tablet, mobile) |
| `onSearchClick` | `MouseEventHandler` | — | Search click |
| `onLogInClick` · `onSignUpClick` | `MouseEventHandler` | — | Signed-out buttons |
| `account` | `ReactNode` | — | Account slot (signed in). Pass your own button + avatar until the DS Avatar ships |
| `accountLabel` | `string` | `'Account'` | Accessible name of the default account button |
| `onAccountClick` | `MouseEventHandler` | — | Default account button click |
| `menuOpen` · `defaultMenuOpen` · `onMenuOpenChange` | `boolean` · `boolean` · `(open) => void` | — · `false` · — | Mobile / tablet menu state |
| `menuLabel` | `string` | `'Menu'` | Menu button accessible name |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<header>` attributes via spread.

# Icon

SVG icon component with lazy loading. Icons load on demand via `React.lazy` and are rendered as React components (`vite-plugin-svgr`).
Size maps to `--size-*` tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Color defaults to `currentColor` so parents (e.g. `IconButton`) control appearance.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Icon } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/icon'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<Icon name="chevron-right" size="lg" />
```

<Canvas of={IconStories.Default} />

---

## Sizes

Nine sizes mapped to CSS tokens:

<Canvas of={IconStories.Sizes} />

| Size | Token | Value |
|------|-------|-------|
| `xs` | `--size-xs` | 12px |
| `sm` | `--size-sm` | 16px |
| `md` | `--size-md` | 20px |
| `lg` | `--size-lg` | 24px |
| `xl` | `--size-xl` | 28px |
| `2xl` | `--size-2xl` | 32px |
| `3xl` | `--size-3xl` | 40px |
| `4xl` | `--size-4xl` | 48px |
| `5xl` | `--size-5xl` | 56px |

| `color` | `string` | `'currentColor'` | CSS color value |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

        buttonStyle="primary"

const VariantSizeMatrix = () => (
  <div style={{ display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: `auto auto repeat(${STATES.length}, 1fr)`, gap: '16px 12px', padding: 24, alignItems: 'center', fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)' }}>
    <div />
    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
    ))}
    {VARIANTS.map((v) =>
      ICON_BUTTON_SIZES.map((size, sizeIdx) => (
        <Row key={`${variantLabel(v)}-${size}`}>
          {sizeIdx === 0 ? (
            <div style={{ gridRow: `span ${ICON_BUTTON_SIZES.length}`, textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{variantLabel(v)}</div>
          ) : null}
          <div>{size}</div>
          {STATES.map((s) => (
            <Cell key={s}>
              <IconButton
                icon="share-2"
                type={v.type}
                buttonStyle={v.buttonStyle}
                size={size}
                aria-label={`${variantLabel(v)} ${size} ${s}`}
                disabled={s === 'disabled'}
                {...forced(s)}
              />
            </Cell>
          ))}
        </Row>

  </div>

Docs doc ok
./components/icon-button/IconButton.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes, Controls } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as IconButtonStories from './IconButton.stories'
import { PlatformCodeTabs } from '../_docs/PlatformCodeTabs'

<Meta of={IconButtonStories} />

# IconButton

A compact button that shows a single icon and no visible label. Use it for actions whose icon is self-evident: search, profile, menu, close, share.
It has four visual types, two style intents (secondary is available on `minimal` only), four sizes and five interaction states.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Icon Button` (7553:28584, doc frame 8349:21817).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { IconButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/icon-button'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<IconButton icon="share-2" aria-label="Share" />
<IconButton icon="x" type="translucid" size="md" aria-label="Close" />
```

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Default} />

<Controls of={IconButtonStories.Default} />

The defaults (`type="minimal"`, `buttonStyle="primary"`, `size="sm"`) keep the 32px icon-only look used across the app (for example in `PodcastSpotlightCard`).

> `type` is the **visual** type from Figma. For the native `<button type>` use `htmlType` (`'button'` by default).

### Cross-platform

<PlatformCodeTabs
  web={`import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
import { IconButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/icon-button'

<IconButton
  icon="player-play-filled"
  type="minimal"
  buttonStyle="primary"
  size="sm"
  aria-label="Play episode"
  onClick={() => player.play()}
/>`}
  ios={`import DailyWireDS
import SwiftUI

// iOS: buttonStyle only for now (no type / size yet)
DWIconButton(
    buttonStyle: .primary,
    accessibilityLabel: "Play episode",
    action: { player.play() }
) {
    Image(systemName: "play.fill")
}`}
  android={`import com.dailywire.ds.DWIconButton
import com.dailywire.ds.DWIconButtonStyle
import com.dailywire.ds.DWIconDefaults
import androidx.compose.material3.Icon
import androidx.compose.material.icons.Icons
import androidx.compose.material.icons.filled.PlayArrow

// Android: buttonStyle only for now (no type / size yet)
DWIconButton(
    buttonStyle = DWIconButtonStyle.Primary,
    contentDescription = "Play episode",
    onClick = { player.play() },
) {
    Icon(
        imageVector = Icons.Filled.PlayArrow,
        contentDescription = null,
        tint = DWIconDefaults.iconColor,
    )
}`}
/>

---

## Types

- **Minimal** — icon only, no surface. Hover and press change the icon color.
- **Ghost** — no surface at rest; a neutral fill appears on hover and press.
- **Outline** — 1px neutral stroke; fill and stroke darken on hover and press.
- **Translucid** — dark glass (`overlay/neutral/strong`, background blur) with an always-light icon, for use over images and video. Looks the same in light and dark.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Types} />

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.TranslucidOnMedia} />

---

## Styles

`buttonStyle` sets the icon color intent. In Figma, **secondary** exists only for the `minimal` type; any other type renders as primary (with a console warning).

| Style | Icon tokens | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `primary` | `icon-neutral-bold-*` | Higher-emphasis actions |
| `secondary` | `icon-neutral-subtlest-*` | Supporting actions (minimal only) |

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

The box is padding + glyph, so it differs per type, as in Figma:

| Size | minimal | ghost / outline / translucid |
|---|---|---|
| `xsm` | 24px · `--spacing-xxs` · glyph `--size-md` | 32px · `--spacing-md` · glyph `--size-sm` |
| `sm` | 32px · `--spacing-xs` · glyph `--size-lg` | 36px · `--spacing-md` · glyph `--size-md` |
| `md` | 40px · `--spacing-md` · glyph `--size-lg` | 40px · `--spacing-md` · glyph `--size-lg` |
| `lg` | 48px · `--spacing-lg` · glyph `--size-lg` | 48px · `--spacing-lg` · glyph `--size-lg` |

Every size is fully round (`--border-radius-full`). On touch screens the hit area grows to `--touch-target-min` (44px) around the visible box without changing layout.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Sizes} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus draws `--focus-ring-width` in `--color-focus-info-bold-default`.
Following Figma, **minimal** and **ghost** draw the ring flush with the circle; **outline** and **translucid** leave a `--focus-ring-offset` gap.

---

## Inspect

Pick type, style, state and size. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.
The panel does not list the icon glyph size (set per size in `IconButton.tsx`), the translucid icon opacity or the background blur; see **Tokens** for those.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every Figma type × style in every state (hover, pressed and focus are forced for review). Use the `size` control to switch sizes.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.States} />

---

## Disabled

The disabled button keeps its `aria-label` and sets `aria-disabled="true"` next to the native `disabled` attribute so screen readers announce it.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.IsDisabled} />

---

## Full matrix

All 100 Figma variants: type × style × size × state.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.VariantSizeMatrix} />

---

## Different icons

Any name from the catalog can be passed to `icon`.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.WithDifferentIcons} />

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Gallery} />

---

## Keyboard interaction

`IconButton` is a native `<button>`: focusable with `Tab`, activated with `Space` and `Enter`.

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Keyboard} />

---

## Accessibility

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| Accessible name | `aria-label` is **required** by TypeScript; axe `button-name` rule enabled |
| Disabled | Native `disabled` + `aria-disabled="true"` |
| Keyboard focus | `:focus-visible` ring, `--focus-ring-width` · `--color-focus-info-bold-default` |
| Touch target | Hit area ≥ `--touch-target-min` on coarse pointers (visible box keeps the Figma size) |
| Motion | 300ms ease-out color/fill transitions (`--duration-slow`, `--easing-ease-out`), removed under `prefers-reduced-motion` |

---

Every property of `IconButton.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

<Canvas of={IconButtonStories.Tokens} />

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={IconButtonStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `icon` | `IconName` | — | Catalog icon (required) |
| `aria-label` | `string` | — | Accessible name (required) |
| `type` | `'minimal' \| 'ghost' \| 'outline' \| 'translucid'` | `'minimal'` | Visual type (Figma `Type`) |
| `buttonStyle` | `'primary' \| 'secondary'` | `'primary'` | Icon color intent; secondary is minimal-only |
| `size` | `'xsm' \| 'sm' \| 'md' \| 'lg'` | `'sm'` | See the size table above |
| `htmlType` | `'button' \| 'submit' \| 'reset'` | `'button'` | Native `<button type>` |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables the button |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

./components/labeled-icon-button/LabeledIconButton.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as LabeledIconButtonStories from './LabeledIconButton.stories'

<Meta of={LabeledIconButtonStories} />

# LabeledIconButton

A compact action that pairs an outlined icon circle with a short label to its right. Use it where an icon alone is not clear enough, but a full-width button would be too heavy (for example "Listen", "Watch", "Share" on a content card).
Spec reviewed against Figma `Libeled Icon Button` (8202:43538, doc frame 8370:15111). It has a single `state` axis.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { LabeledIconButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/labeled-icon-button'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<LabeledIconButton icon="headphones" label="Listen" onClick={play} />
```

<Canvas of={LabeledIconButtonStories.Default} />

<Canvas of={LabeledIconButtonStories.Examples} />

---

## Anatomy

- **Circle** — the Figma `Icon Button` (outline · xsm): 32px, `--spacing-md` padding, 16px glyph (`--size-sm`), 1px inside stroke, `--border-radius-full`.
- **Label** — text style `label/600/sm` (`--font-size-xs`, `--Semibold`, `--line-height-100`), single line.
- **Gap** — `--spacing-sm` (6px).

The whole row is **one** `<button>`. The circle is drawn inside it with the same tokens as `IconButton` (outline) rather than nesting an `IconButton`, because a button cannot contain another button. Hover, press and focus apply to the unit: pointing at the label also fills the circle, and the focus ring wraps both.
The label is the accessible name, so no `aria-label` is needed.

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus draws `--focus-ring-width` in `--color-focus-info-bold-default` around the whole component, with a `--focus-ring-offset` gap and `--border-radius-sm` corners.

---

## Inspect

Pick a state. The component is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state (rows marked `· circle` act on the icon circle) — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={LabeledIconButtonStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every Figma state (hover, pressed and focus are forced for review):

<Canvas of={LabeledIconButtonStories.States} />

| State | Label | Circle fill | Circle stroke | Icon |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Default | `text-neutral-bold-default` | — | `border-neutral-subtle-default` | `icon-neutral-bold-default` |
| Hover | `text-neutral-bold-hovered` | `background-neutral-bold-hovered` | `border-neutral-bold-hovered` | `icon-neutral-bold-default` |
| Pressed | `text-neutral-bold-pressed` | `background-neutral-bold-pressed` | `border-neutral-bold-pressed` | `icon-neutral-bold-default` |
| Focus | `text-neutral-bold-default` | — | `border-neutral-subtle-default` | `icon-neutral-bold-default` |
| Disabled | `text-neutral-bold-disabled` | — | `border-neutral-bold-disabled` | `icon-neutral-bold-disabled` |

---

Native `disabled` plus `aria-disabled="true"`; clicks are ignored.

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| Accessible name | The visible `label` |
| Keyboard | Native `<button>`: `Tab` to focus, `Space` / `Enter` to activate |
| Focus | `:focus-visible` ring around the whole unit |
| Touch target | The row is 32px tall; on coarse pointers the hit area grows to `--touch-target-min` (44px) without changing layout |
| Motion | 300ms ease-out color transitions (`--duration-slow`, `--easing-ease-out`), removed under `prefers-reduced-motion` |

    <div style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 24 }}>
      <Link href="#" variant="primary" underline>Underline on</Link>
      <Link href="#" variant="secondary" underline>Underline on</Link>
    </div>
    <div style={{ display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: 24 }}>
      <Link href="#" variant="primary" underline={false}>Underline off</Link>
      <Link href="#" variant="secondary" underline={false}>Underline off</Link>
    </div>
  </ShowcaseBox>
);

States story ok
Every style × weight × underline in every Figma state. Hover and focus are forced with `data-state`.
const States = ({ size, leadingIcon, trailingIcon }) => (
  <div style={gridStyle(STATES.length)}>
    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
    ))}
    {LINK_VARIANTS.flatMap((variant) =>
      LINK_WEIGHTS.flatMap((weight) =>
        [true, false].map((underline) => (
          <Row key={`${variant}-${weight}-${underline}`}>
            <div style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{`${variant} · ${weight} · underline ${underline ? 'on' : 'off'}`}</div>
            {STATES.map((s) => (
              <Cell key={s}>
                <Link
                  href="#"
                  variant={variant}
                  size={size}
                  weight={weight}
                  underline={underline}
                  leadingIcon={leadingIcon}
                  trailingIcon={trailingIcon}
                  {...stateProps(s)}
                >
                  Link Button
                </Link>
              </Cell>
            ))}
          </Row>
        )),
      ),
    )}
  </div>
);
Figma Matrix story ok
The full Figma set (96 variants): style × weight × underline rows, state × size columns.
const FigmaMatrix = () => (
  <div style={gridStyle(STATES.length * LINK_SIZES.length)}>
    <div />
    {STATES.flatMap((s) =>
      [...LINK_SIZES].reverse().map((size) => (
        <div key={`${s}-${size}`} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{`${s} · ${size}`}</div>
      )),
    )}
    {LINK_VARIANTS.flatMap((variant) =>
      LINK_WEIGHTS.flatMap((weight) =>
        [true, false].map((underline) => (
          <Row key={`${variant}-${weight}-${underline}`}>
            <div style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{`${variant} · ${weight} · ${underline ? 'on' : 'off'}`}</div>
            {STATES.flatMap((s) =>
              [...LINK_SIZES].reverse().map((size) => (
                <Cell key={`${s}-${size}`}>
                  <Link
                    href="#"
                    variant={variant}
                    size={size}
                    weight={weight}
                    underline={underline}
                    leadingIcon="chevron-left"
                    trailingIcon="chevron-right"
                    {...stateProps(s)}
                  >
                    Link Button
                  </Link>
                </Cell>
              )),
            )}
          </Row>
        )),
      ),
    )}
  </div>
);

Docs doc ok
./components/link/Link.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as LinkStories from './Link.stories'

<Meta of={LinkStories} />

# Link

Inline navigation styled as text, with optional leading and trailing icons. Unlike `Button`, a link has no container, fill or border in any state: it shows interactivity through text color and an optional underline.
Two styles, three sizes, two weights, underline on or off, and four states (default, hover, focus, disabled).
Spec from Figma `Link Button` (7797:24559, docs frame 8370:15678). Link is its own component, not a Button variant.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Link } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/link'

<Link href="/shows" variant="primary" trailingIcon="chevron-right">
  All shows
</Link>
```

Renders a native `<a>`. For an action that does not navigate (open a dialog, expand content), use `as="button"`: it renders a native `<button type="button">` with the same look.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={LinkStories.Default} />

---

## Styles

Figma's `style` axis is the `variant` prop:

- **Primary** — `text/neutral/bold` and `icon/neutral/bold` tokens: the strongest link.
- **Secondary** — `text/neutral/subtlest` and `icon/neutral/subtlest` tokens: quieter, for supporting links.

<Canvas of={LinkStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

Three sizes, set by text style and icon size. The box hugs its content (no padding), so its height is the tallest child:

| Size | Text style | Font size | Icon | Height |
|------|-----------|-----------|------|--------|
| `lg` | `label/400/lg` · `label/600/lg` | `--font-size-md` (16) | `--size-md` (20) | 20 |
| `md` | `label/400/md` · `label/600/md` | `--font-size-sm` (14) | `--size-sm` (16) | 16 |
| `sm` | `label/400/sm` · `label/600/sm` | `--font-size-xs` (12) | `--size-sm` (16) | 16 |

Icon to label gap is `--spacing-xs` on every size.

<Canvas of={LinkStories.Sizes} />

---

## Weight

`regular` uses the `label/400/*` text styles (`--Regular`), `semibold` uses `label/600/*` (`--Semibold`).

<Canvas of={LinkStories.Weights} />

---

## Underline

On by default. The underline sits under the label only, never under the icons, and stays the same in every state (hover does not add or remove it). Turn it off for links whose role is obvious from context, such as navigation rows.

<Canvas of={LinkStories.Underline} />

---

## With icons

Figma's `Show icon left` and `Show icon right` booleans are the `leadingIcon` and `trailingIcon` props, which take any name from the icon catalog. Figma's default glyphs are `chevron-left` and `chevron-right`.

<Canvas of={LinkStories.WithIcons} />

---

## In running text

Pick the size that matches the surrounding paragraph.

<Canvas of={LinkStories.InText} />

---

## As a button

<Canvas of={LinkStories.AsButton} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus shows the same ring on both styles: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset` and `--border-radius-sm` corners. The label and icons switch to their `selected` tokens.

---

## Disabled

A disabled link renders `aria-disabled="true"` and `role="link"`, drops its `href` (so it cannot navigate or take focus) and ignores clicks. With `as="button"`, the native `disabled` attribute is set as well.

<Canvas of={LinkStories.IsDisabled} />

---

## Inspect

Pick style, state, size, weight, underline and icons. The link is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state. Hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={LinkStories.Inspect} />

Every style × weight × underline in every Figma state (hover and focus are forced for review):

All 96 Figma variants: style × weight × underline × state × size.

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={LinkStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `'primary' \| 'secondary'` | `'secondary'` | Figma `style`: neutral-bold or neutral-subtlest tokens |
| `size` | `'sm' \| 'md' \| 'lg'` | `'lg'` | Text style and icon size (12/14/16 px text) |
| `weight` | `'regular' \| 'semibold'` | `'regular'` | `label/400/*` or `label/600/*` |
| `underline` | `boolean` | `true` | Underline the label |
| `leadingIcon` | `IconName` | — | Icon before the label |
| `trailingIcon` | `IconName` | — | Icon after the label |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | `aria-disabled`, no `href`, clicks blocked |
| `as` | `'a' \| 'button'` | `'a'` | Native element to render |
| `type` | `'button' \| 'submit' \| 'reset'` | `'button'` | Button type, only with `as="button"` |
| `href` | `string` | — | Link target |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Label |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<a>` attributes via spread (`target`, `rel`, `onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

Docs doc ok
./components/live-blog-post/LiveBlogPost.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as PostStories from './LiveBlogPost.stories'

<Meta of={PostStories} />

# LiveBlogPost

One entry of a live blog: an optional "Pinned" marker, the author row (avatar, timestamp, byline) with a share button, the headline, optional media (image, video, or a tweet / YouTube embed slot) and a body that opens with "Show more".
Spec from Figma `Live Blog Article` (10033:13086) in the "Article" section of the "↳ Content" page: `CardState` Closed | Opened × `Content` VerticalVideo | Text | Image | Twitter Post | HorizontalVideo | Youtube video × `Breakpoint` Mobile | Tablet | Desktop × `Type` Default | Pinned, plus `Show Avatar` (72 variants).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { LiveBlogPost } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/live-blog-post'

<LiveBlogPost
  title="The One Line In Iran’s Proposal That Made Trump ‘Throw It Away’"
  timestamp="3 min ago"
  dateTime="2026-11-05T14:57:00Z"
  author="Ben Shapiro"
  authorHref="/author/ben-shapiro"
  avatar={{ src: '/hosts/ben-shapiro.jpg' }}
  content="image"
  imageSrc="/media/tehran-rally.jpg"
  imageAlt="A cleric in a black turban walks through a crowd"
  onShare={() => navigator.share?.({ url: '/live/iran#post-123' })}
>
  <p>President Trump said Thursday that he immediately rejected Iran’s latest proposal…</p>
  <p>Speaking to reporters, Trump said the proposal failed to satisfy his demand…</p>
</LiveBlogPost>
```

Built from DS parts: `Avatar` (Hosts · 2xsm / xsm), `Byline` (secondary · sm / md, no avatar), `IconButton` (outline · share-2 · xsm / md), `Link` (secondary · md · underline, as a `<button>`) and `ArticleVideo` (the video content). The filled pin is `icons/pinned-filled.svg`, exported from the Figma frame.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={PostStories.Default} />

---

## Content

Figma's `Content` axis is the `content` prop. It picks what sits between the headline and the body:

| `content` | Figma | Renders |
|-----------|-------|---------|
| `text` | Text | No media |
| `image` | Image | `imageSrc` / `imageAlt` as an `<img>` in a 16:9 box (cover), optional `imageCaption` (`<figcaption>`, not drawn in Figma) |
| `vertical-video` | VerticalVideo | `ArticleVideo` with the `video` props, 2:3 box (the Figma frames are 393 × 606 · 640 × 965 · 672 × 1003) |
| `horizontal-video` | HorizontalVideo | `ArticleVideo` with the `video` props, 16:9 box |
| `twitter` | Twitter Post | The `embed` slot, inside the text gutter |
| `youtube` | Youtube video | The `embed` slot in a 16:9 frame; its child is stretched to fill it |

`video.aspect` overrides the video ratio (e.g. `'9/16'` for a full-height short).

<Canvas of={PostStories.Content} />

### Embeds (Twitter / X and YouTube)

The component **never loads third-party scripts or iframes**. Pass your own embed in `embed`: a click-to-load facade, a server-rendered card, or an iframe your app is allowed to load. The stories use static placeholders built from tokens: a tweet card (Figma pins it to the Light color mode, so it sets `data-theme="light"`) with a "View on X" link, and a still of the player with a "Watch on YouTube" link.

<Canvas of={PostStories.ImageCaption} />

---

## Type: Pinned

`pinned` is Figma `Type=Pinned`: a "Pinned" marker (`pinnedLabel`, filled pin, subtlest label/500/md · lg) above the author row, the `--color-background-neutral-subtle-default` surface and no bottom stroke. On Desktop the pinned post becomes a card: `--border-radius-lg` corners and its own `--spacing-4xl` gutter, with image and video running edge to edge.

<Canvas of={PostStories.Pinned} />

---

## CardState: Closed / Opened

The only difference between Figma's Closed and Opened is the body: Closed clamps it to **10 lines** on Mobile and **8 lines** from 640px, with an ellipsis and "Show more"; Opened shows all of it with "Show less". Headline and media do not change.

- Uncontrolled: `defaultExpanded`. Controlled: `expanded` + `onExpandedChange`.
- The toggle is the DS `Link` rendered as a `<button>` with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` pointing at the body.
- While closed, the toggle only appears when the clamp actually hides text.

<Canvas of={PostStories.CardStates} />

<Canvas of={PostStories.Controlled} />

---

## Show Avatar

`showAvatar` (default `true`) is Figma `Show Avatar`: the author avatar before the timestamp and name. It is decorative (the author name follows it).

<Canvas of={PostStories.WithoutAvatar} />

---

## Breakpoints

Figma's `Breakpoint` axis is **not a prop**: the post switches with CSS container queries on its own width. The Figma frames are Mobile 393, **Desktop 640** and **Tablet 768**: Desktop is the 640px live-blog column of a desktop page (the page supplies the gutters), Tablet is a full-width post that draws its own gutters. `tokens.css` has no breakpoint tokens, so the limits are the Figma frame widths:

| Breakpoint | Post width | Text gutter | Image / video / YouTube | Headline · body | Avatar · share | Closed body |
|------------|------------|-------------|-------------------------|-----------------|----------------|-------------|
| Mobile | < 640 | `--spacing-xl` | Edge to edge, square corners | heading/500/md · body/400/md | 32 (`2xsm`) · 32 (`xsm`) | 10 lines |
| Desktop | 640 – 767 | none (Pinned: `--spacing-4xl`) | Full width, `--border-radius-md` (Pinned: edge to edge, square) | heading/500/lg · body/400/lg | 40 (`xsm`) · 40 (`md`) | 8 lines |
| Tablet | ≥ 768 | `--spacing-6xl` | Inside the gutter, `--border-radius-md` | heading/500/lg · body/400/md | 40 (`xsm`) · 40 (`md`) | 8 lines |

From 640px, image, horizontal video, YouTube and tweet posts space their sections `--spacing-4xl` apart (`--spacing-3xl` otherwise, as in Figma). The avatar, byline and share button switch DS size at 640px: one copy per range is rendered and the other is `display: none`, so only one is ever in the accessibility tree.

<Canvas of={PostStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Inspect

Pick Content, CardState, Type, Breakpoint and Show Avatar. The post is drawn at the frame width (scaled to fit) with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply at that width, including the container-query rules.

<Canvas of={PostStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every Figma variant at one breakpoint: Content (rows) × CardState · Type (columns). Switch the breakpoint in Controls.

<Canvas of={PostStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `LiveBlogPost.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme: the shared (Mobile) rules first, then each breakpoint range. `Avatar`, `Byline`, `IconButton`, `Link` and `ArticleVideo` document their own tokens.

<Canvas of={PostStories.Tokens} />

---

## Accessibility

- The root is an `<article>` named by its headline (`aria-labelledby`). The headline level is `headingLevel` (default `h2`: the live blog page owns the `h1`).
- The timestamp is a `<time dateTime>` when `dateTime` is set.
- Share is an `IconButton` named by `shareLabel` ("Share"); it is rendered only when `onShare` is set. On touch screens its hit area grows to `--touch-target-min`.
- "Show more" / "Show less" is a `<button>` with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`; its hit area also grows to `--touch-target-min` on touch screens. The clamp is visual only: screen readers read the whole body in both states.
- Images need a real `imageAlt`; videos a `video.label`. Embeds are never fetched by the component.

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={PostStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `title` | `ReactNode` | — | Headline |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5 \| 6` | `2` | Headline element |
| `timestamp` · `dateTime` | `string` · `string` | — | Visible time ("3 min ago") and its ISO date for `<time>` |
| `author` · `authorHref` | `string` · `string` | — | Byline name and optional author page |
| `avatar` · `showAvatar` | `{ src?, type?, initials? }` · `boolean` | — · `true` | Author avatar (Figma `Show Avatar`) |
| `pinned` · `pinnedLabel` | `boolean` · `string` | `false` · `'Pinned'` | Figma `Type=Pinned` |
| `content` | `'vertical-video' \| 'text' \| 'image' \| 'twitter' \| 'horizontal-video' \| 'youtube'` | `'text'` | Figma `Content` |
| `imageSrc` · `imageAlt` · `imageCaption` | `string` · `string` · `ReactNode` | — · `''` · — | `content="image"` |
| `video` | `ArticleVideoProps` | — | `content="vertical-video" \| "horizontal-video"` |
| `embed` | `ReactNode` | — | `content="twitter" \| "youtube"` |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Body (paragraphs) |
| `expanded` · `defaultExpanded` · `onExpandedChange` | `boolean` · `boolean` · `(expanded) => void` | — · `false` · — | Figma `CardState` (Opened = `true`) |
| `showMoreLabel` · `showLessLabel` | `string` | `'Show more'` · `'Show less'` | Disclosure labels |
| `onShare` · `shareLabel` | `(event) => void` · `string` | — · `'Share'` | Share button |
| `className` · `style` | — | — | Root `<article>` |

> `ref` points at the root `<article>`. Other `<article>` attributes are forwarded.

./components/live-updates-block/LiveUpdatesBlock.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as LiveUpdatesBlockStories from './LiveUpdatesBlock.stories'

# LiveUpdatesBlock

The live coverage module of a news page: a "Live Updates" header, the lead story, the media slider, the latest updates as a timeline and a link to all of them.
Three Figma breakpoints (Desktop, Tablet, Mobile), switched by the block's own width.
Spec from Figma `Live Updates block` (9113:51726, doc frame 9160:66974), section "Article" on page "↳ Content". It nests the DS `ArticleBlock`, `LiveUpdatesSlider` (Figma "Slider Live Updates"), `SummaryNews` and `Link` (Figma "Link Button"). Figma's `SectionHeader` and `StatusDot` are not DS components: the default header is drawn inside this component, and `header` replaces it.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { LiveUpdatesBlock } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/live-updates-block'

<LiveUpdatesBlock
  timestamp={new Date(story.updatedAt)}
  formatTimestamp={timeAgo}
  article={{ href: story.url, title: story.title, description: story.standfirst, onMoreClick: openMenu }}
  slides={story.media.map((m) => ({ type: 'image', src: m.url, alt: m.alt }))}
  updates={story.updates.map((u) => ({ id: u.id, href: u.url, publishDate: new Date(u.at), formatDate: timeAgo, title: u.headline }))}
  viewAllHref={`${story.url}#updates`}
/>
```

Renders a `<section>` named by its header label. Headings: the label is an `h2` (`headingLevel`), the lead title an `h3`, each update an `h4`.

---

## Usage

The block fills its container; resize the canvas to see it switch layouts.

<Canvas of={LiveUpdatesBlockStories.Default} />

---

## Anatomy

| Part | Component | Figma |
|------|-----------|-------|
| Header | private (`header` slot replaces it) | SectionHeader · static · live · sm · Show Timestamp: live dot, label, bullet, timestamp |
| Lead story | `ArticleBlock` (`article` prop) | ArticleBlock · xl (lg on mobile) · Show Metadata off |
| Media | `LiveUpdatesSlider` (`slides`, `sliderProps`) | Slider Live Updates · Content 3 |
| Updates | `SummaryNews` (`updates`) | Summary news |
| View all | `Link` primary · md · semibold · underline · `arrow-right` (`viewAllHref`) | Link Button "View All Updates" |

**Default header** — a 16px box (`--size-sm`) with an 8px `icon-live-bold-default` dot (`--size-2xs`, Figma StatusDot · live · md), then the label (`label/600/md`, `text-neutral-bold-default`, the heading), a bullet (`label/400/md`, `text-neutral-subtlest-default`, hidden from assistive technology) and the timestamp (`label/600/sm`, `text-brand-bold-default`, a `<time>`), `--spacing-sm` apart. `timestamp={null}` hides the bullet and timestamp (Figma Show Timestamp off).

---

## Breakpoints

Figma's `Breakpoint` axis is responsive CSS: container queries on the block's own width, with the Figma frame widths as limits (tokens.css has no breakpoint tokens).

| Breakpoint | Width | Layout |
|---|---|---|
| Mobile | < 768 | One column: lead (`--spacing-4xl` top and bottom, `--spacing-xl` sides) · slider full bleed · updates (`--spacing-xl` sides, `--spacing-3xl` above, between and below). ArticleBlock `lg`. |
| Tablet | 768 – 1375 | Mobile layout with `--spacing-6xl` sides. ArticleBlock `xl`. |
| Desktop | ≥ 1376 | Lead, updates and link in a column beside the slider (480 : 864, the Figma widths at 1376), `--spacing-4xl` gaps, no outer padding. ArticleBlock `xl`. |

The slider picks its own ratio and corners from its width (rounded from 864px). Desktop starts at 1376px so the slider always gets its desktop size beside the lead column. The ArticleBlock size is a prop, so the block renders an `xl` and an `lg` copy and shows one per width (`display: none` keeps the other out of the accessibility tree and the tab order). For that reason `article` takes no `id`.

<Canvas of={LiveUpdatesBlockStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Header slot

`header` replaces the default header. Name the section yourself then, with `aria-labelledby` or `aria-label`.

<Canvas of={LiveUpdatesBlockStories.CustomHeader} />

---

## Data

Every text is a prop with the Figma text as default: `label` ("Live Updates"), `timestamp` ("1m ago"), the `article` title and description, the `updates` and `viewAllLabel` ("View All Updates"). `slides` is required (images, videos or custom content; no default media). Without `viewAllHref` the link is not drawn. A `Date` `timestamp` fills `<time dateTime>` and goes through `formatTimestamp`.

<Canvas of={LiveUpdatesBlockStories.WithData} />

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={LiveUpdatesBlockStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `slides` | `LiveUpdatesSlide[]` | — (required) | Slider media: `{ type: 'image', src, alt }`, `{ type: 'video', … }`, `{ type: 'custom', content }` |
| `sliderProps` | `Partial<LiveUpdatesSliderProps>` | — | `defaultIndex`, `index`, `onIndexChange`, `label`… |
| `article` | `Partial<ArticleBlockProps>` (no `size`, `headingAs`, `id`) | Figma title + description | The lead story: `href`, `title`, `description`, `metadata`, `onMoreClick`… |
| `updates` | `SummaryNewsItem[]` | Figma's three entries | The latest updates, newest first |
| `header` | `ReactNode` | — | Replaces the default header |
| `label` | `string` | `'Live Updates'` | Default header label (the section's name) |
| `timestamp` | `string \| Date \| null` | `'1m ago'` | Default header timestamp; `null` hides it |
| `timestampDateTime` | `string` | ISO of a `Date` | `<time dateTime>` |
| `formatTimestamp` | `(date: Date) => string` | "3:45 PM" | Formats a `Date` `timestamp` |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5 \| 6` | `2` | Label level; lead title +1, updates +2 (max h6) |
| `viewAllHref` | `string` | — | "View All Updates" target; omit to hide the link |
| `viewAllLabel` | `string` | `'View All Updates'` | Link text |
| `viewAllProps` | `Partial<LinkProps>` | — | Extra `Link` props |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class on the root |

> Also accepts native `<section>` attributes via spread (`id`, `aria-*`, `data-*`, etc.).

# LiveUpdatesSlider

The media carousel of a live updates block: one slide at a time (images, a video, or any node), previous / next arrows and position dots.
Spec from Figma `Slider Live Updates` (9096:6145) in the "Article" section of the "↳ Content" page: `Content` 1 | 2 | 3 × `Breakpoint` Mobile | Tablet | Desktop (9 variants).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark; the overlay controls look the same in both themes.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { LiveUpdatesSlider } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/live-updates-slider'

<LiveUpdatesSlider
  label="Iran strikes: photos and video"
  slides={[
    { type: 'image', src: '/media/launch.jpg', alt: 'A missile lifts off from its launch pad' },
    { type: 'image', src: '/media/stamps.jpg', alt: 'Collage of torn Iranian stamps' },
    { type: 'video', src: '/media/skyline.mp4', poster: '/media/skyline.jpg', label: 'Explosions light up the skyline' },
  ]}
/>
```

Built from DS parts: `IconButton` (translucid · lg: chevrons) and `ArticleVideo` (video slides, with its own mute toggle).

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={SliderStories.Default} />

---

## Content

Figma's `Content 1 / 2 / 3` is the **active slide** of a three-slide set (image, image, video), not a prop of its own: use `index` (controlled) or `defaultIndex`. Previous is disabled on the first slide and next on the last one (no wrap-around, no auto-rotation, as in Figma). On the video slide the volume button top-right is the `ArticleVideo` mute toggle. Leaving a slide pauses its video.

<Canvas of={SliderStories.Content} />

### Slides

`slides` is an array of:

- `{ type: 'image', src, alt }`: a picture covering the slide (Figma image fill, cover).
- `{ type: 'video', ...ArticleVideoProps }`: an `ArticleVideo` stretched to the slide (`aspect="fill"`).
- `{ type: 'custom', content }`: any node stretched to the slide, e.g. a placeholder for an embed (embeds are never fetched).

<Canvas of={SliderStories.CustomSlide} />

---

## Breakpoints

Figma's `Breakpoint` axis is **not a prop**. The slider switches with CSS container queries on its own width. `tokens.css` has no breakpoint or ratio tokens, so the limits and ratios are the Figma frame sizes:

| Breakpoint | Slider width | Ratio (Figma frame) | Corners |
|------------|--------------|---------------------|---------|
| Mobile | < 768 | 393 × 470 | `--border-radius-none` |
| Tablet | 768 – 863 | 768 × 627 | `--border-radius-none` |
| Desktop | ≥ 864 | 864 × 547 | `--border-radius-md` |

On every breakpoint the arrows sit `--spacing-xl` from the bottom-right corner (`--spacing-sm` apart) and the dots pill is centered on the frame, on the arrows' center line.

<Canvas of={SliderStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Swipe

Figma's prototype changes slide on drag. On touch and pen, a horizontal swipe over 15 % of the slide width goes to the next / previous slide; vertical swipes keep scrolling the page. Mouse drags are ignored so a click on a video still plays / pauses.

---

## Controlled

<Canvas of={SliderStories.Controlled} />

---

## Inspect

Pick Content and Breakpoint. The slider is drawn at the frame width (scaled to fit) with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply at that width, including the container-query rules.

<Canvas of={SliderStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

All 9 Figma variants, at the Figma frame widths:

<Canvas of={SliderStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `LiveUpdatesSlider.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme: the shared (Mobile) rules first, then each breakpoint range. `IconButton` and `ArticleVideo` document their own tokens.

<Canvas of={SliderStories.Tokens} />

---

## Accessibility

Follows the WAI-ARIA APG carousel pattern (no auto-rotation):

- The root is a `<section>` region with `aria-roledescription="carousel"`, named by `label` ("Live updates").
- Each slide is a `group` with `aria-roledescription="slide"` named "n of m" (`slideLabel`). Hidden slides are `aria-hidden` and `inert`, so their controls leave the tab order.
- The slides container is `aria-live="polite"`: moving with the arrows announces the new slide.
- Previous / next come first in reading order, named by `previousLabel` / `nextLabel` and pointing at the slides with `aria-controls`. When one gets disabled at either end, focus moves to the other.
- The dots are decorative (`aria-hidden`); position is in the slide names.
- Image slides need a real `alt`. Transitions stop under `prefers-reduced-motion`.

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={SliderStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `slides` | `LiveUpdatesSlide[]` | — | `image` · `video` · `custom` slides, in order |
| `index` · `defaultIndex` · `onIndexChange` | `number` · `number` · `(index) => void` | — · `0` · — | Active slide, 0-based (Figma `Content`) |
| `label` | `string` | `'Live updates'` | Name of the carousel region |
| `previousLabel` · `nextLabel` | `string` | `'Previous slide'` · `'Next slide'` | Arrow names |
| `slideLabel` | `(position, total) => string` | `` `${position} of ${total}` `` | Slide names |
| `className` · `style` | — | — | Root `<section>` |

> `ref` points at the root `<section>`. Other `<section>` attributes are forwarded.
