# LiveUpdateSummary

One entry of a live coverage feed: when it was posted, then a short headline. With `href` the whole entry is one link.
Three Figma states (Default, hover, pressed) plus keyboard focus.
Spec from Figma `Live Updates Article Summary` (9133:58532, doc frame 9158:64719), section "Article" on page "↳ Content". It nests the DS `ArticleMetadata` (Figma "Metadata": Type Inverted, size sm, Publish Date only) and `ArticleHeading` (Figma "Heading-Article-Content": size xsm, weight medium). Through the metadata row it can also show `Byline` / `Avatar` and a `LabeledIconButton` action.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { LiveUpdateSummary } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/live-update-summary'

<LiveUpdateSummary
  href="/live/oil-prices#update-42"
  publishDate={new Date(update.publishedAt)}
  formatDate={timeAgo}
  title="New report shows glaciers melting faster than previously thought, experts say"
/>
```

Renders an `<article>`: the `ArticleMetadata` row (the date as `<time>`), then the headline as an `h3` (`headingLevel` changes it).

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={LiveUpdateSummaryStories.Default} />

---

## Anatomy

| Part | Component | Figma |
|------|-----------|-------|
| Date | `ArticleMetadata` `variant="inverted"` `size="sm"` | Metadata · Type Inverted · size sm · Publish Date only. Override: `text-neutral-bold-default` (the row's default is subtlest). |
| Headline | `ArticleHeading` `size="xsm"` `weight="medium"` | Heading-Article-Content · `title/500/sm` (14px, 120%) |

The two parts sit `--spacing-md` (8px) apart in a column. The entry is fluid; Figma draws it at 348px.

---

## Link

With `href`, the headline is the entry's only link and its hit area is stretched over the whole entry (`ArticleHeading` `stretched`): the accessible card pattern, one `<a>` whose name is the headline. Hovering or pressing anywhere on the entry recolors the date and the headline together. Extra `<a>` attributes go in `linkProps`.

Without `href` the entry is static text:

<Canvas of={LiveUpdateSummaryStories.WithoutLink} />

---

## Data

`publishDate` takes a string ("10 min ago") or a `Date`. A `Date` fills `<time dateTime>` with its ISO string and is shown through `formatDate` (default "Nov 5, 2026"); pass a relative formatter for "2 hours ago".

<Canvas of={LiveUpdateSummaryStories.WithData} />

`metadataProps` reaches the rest of `ArticleMetadata` (author, read time, an action). Items without a value are not drawn, so by default the row is the date alone, as in Figma. A linked author or an action sits above the stretched link and stays clickable.

<Canvas of={LiveUpdateSummaryStories.WithAuthor} />

A plain stack of entries (the timeline version with status dots is `SummaryNews`):

<Canvas of={LiveUpdateSummaryStories.Feed} />

---

The Figma states plus keyboard focus (forced for review with `data-state`):

<Canvas of={LiveUpdateSummaryStories.States} />

| State | Date | Headline | Ring |
|---|---|---|---|
| Default | `text-neutral-bold-default` | `text-neutral-bold-default` | — |
| Hover | `text-neutral-bold-hovered` | `text-neutral-bold-hovered` | — |
| Pressed | `text-neutral-bold-pressed` | `text-neutral-bold-pressed` | — |
| Focus | `text-neutral-bold-default` | `text-neutral-bold-default` | `focus-info-bold-default` around the headline (ArticleHeading) |

Figma's hover is a 600ms dissolve, ease-out: both colors change over `--duration-slower` (closest token) with `--easing-ease-out`, and not at all under `prefers-reduced-motion`.

<ArgTypes of={LiveUpdateSummaryStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `title` | `ReactNode` | Figma headline | The headline (Figma "Headline") |
| `href` | `string` | — | Makes the entry one link (stretched headline link) |

# Loader

The system's indeterminate loading indicator: three dots that signal work in progress without implying how long it will take.
It is designed to be dropped inside other components (buttons, players, lists) rather than used on its own.
Nothing moves — the dots sit at fixed positions and only their opacity animates, a highlight travelling left to right — so it never shifts the surrounding layout.

Spec reviewed against Figma `Loader / Dots` (13455:15617, doc frame 13529:213291), 9 variants: Color × Frame.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { Loader } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/loader'
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={LoaderStories.Default} />

```tsx
<Loader label="Loading episodes" />
<Loader color="always-light" label="Loading video" />
```

The root is a `role="status"` region; `label` is announced to screen readers and hidden visually. The dots are `aria-hidden`.

---

## Colors

- **Default** — `icon/neutral/bold`; follows the theme (dark dots in Light, light dots in Dark).
- **Always light** — `icon/always/light`; on imagery, video or brand surfaces.
- **Inverse** — `icon/inverse/bold`; on inverse surfaces (e.g. inside a primary button).

<Canvas of={LoaderStories.Colors} />

---

## Animation

Figma prototype: *After delay 10ms → Smart Animate 280ms, ease-in-out*, Frame 1 → 2 → 3 → 1. One loop is 3 × (10 + 280) = **870ms**; the lit dot is at opacity 1 and the others at 0.3.
Code uses one `@keyframes` with the dots offset by −580ms / −290ms — the same curve as the `Button` loading state.
With `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the loop slows to 2.6s.

<Canvas of={LoaderStories.InContext} />

---

## Inspect

Pick color and frame. The loader is drawn at 2× with its real measurements (36×16, 8px dots, 6px apart); the panel lists the tokens that apply.

<Canvas of={LoaderStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every Figma variant — Color × Frame 1 / 2 / 3 (frozen with `data-frame`) — plus the running loop:

<Canvas of={LoaderStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `Loader.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

<Canvas of={LoaderStories.Tokens} />

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={LoaderStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `'default' \| 'always-light' \| 'inverse'` | `'default'` | Dot color |
| `label` | `string` | `'Loading'` | Screen-reader text of the status region |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<span>` attributes via spread. `data-frame="1" | "2" | "3"` freezes a Figma frame (docs / visual QA only).

# Media Swap Button

Audio ↔ video toggle for content that can be watched or listened to. Two segments sit in a pill; the selected one is filled.
Each segment is a toggle button (`aria-pressed`) inside a `fieldset` group named "Playback mode" (override with `aria-label`).
Spec reviewed against Figma `Swap_Video` (5599:8341), variant `Type` = Video / Audio.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

> **Legacy source.** Figma binds old variables and raw spacing. Mapping:
> `Background/Light Container` (#1f1f1f) → `--color-background-neutral-subtlest-default` (track);
> `Background/Base` (#0a0909) → `--color-background-neutral-bold-default` (selected segment);
> `Text/Default/Base` → `--color-text-neutral-bold-default`; `Text/Default/Strong` (#8f8f8f) → `--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default`;
> icons are `Icon/Strong` / `Icon/Solid` at 30% opacity → `--color-icon-neutral-subtlest-default` / `-disabled` (no opacity token);
> padding 4 / 4·8, gap 4 → `--spacing-xs` / `--spacing-md`; radius 200 / 100 → `--border-radius-full`.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { MediaSwapButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/media-swap-button'
```

---

## Usage

Uncontrolled — click the other segment to swap:

<Canvas of={MediaSwapStories.Default} />

Controlled with `mode` and `onModeChange`:

<Canvas of={MediaSwapStories.Controlled} />

## Modes

Figma variants `Type = Video` and `Type = Audio` map to `mode="video"` and `mode="audio"`. Icons are `video` and `headphones` (20px).

<Canvas of={MediaSwapStories.Modes} />

---

## Size

One size: 4px track padding, segments 4 / 8 padding with `body/400/sm` text (37px tall overall). An invisible area extends each segment's touch target to `--touch-target-min` (44px).

---

## States

Figma draws only the two selections. Hover and pressed on the unselected segment, and disabled, use the neutral-subtlest family.
Hover, pressed and focus are forced for review (focus rings the selected segment):

<Canvas of={MediaSwapStories.States} />

### Focus

Each segment is focusable and shows the DS ring: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset`.

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={MediaSwapStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `mode` | `'video' \| 'audio'` | — | Selected mode, controlled (Figma `Type`) |
| `defaultMode` | `'video' \| 'audio'` | `'video'` | Initial mode when `mode` is not controlled |
| `onModeChange` | `(mode) => void` | — | Called when the user picks the other segment |
| `videoLabel` | `string` | `'Video'` | Video segment text |
| `audioLabel` | `string` | `'Audio'` | Audio segment text |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables both segments |
| `aria-label` | `string` | `'Playback mode'` | Accessible name of the group |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<fieldset>` attributes via spread.

Docs doc ok
./components/native-ad-carousel-card/NativeAdCarouselCard.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes, Controls } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as CardStories from './NativeAdCarouselCard.stories'

<Meta of={CardStories} />

# NativeAdCarouselCard

A sponsored card for article carousels: creative (image or muted video), title, a "Sponsored by …" byline and an optional call to action.
One component covers two Figma sets — `Native Ad Carousel EP (Web)` (12761:19853, 10 variants) and `Native Ad Carousel EP (App)` (12761:19950, 6 variants) — through the `layout` prop.
Section "Article" (8413:56868), page "↳ Content". All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { NativeAdCarouselCard } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/native-ad-carousel-card'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<NativeAdCarouselCard
  media={{ type: 'image', src: creative.url }}
  href="https://advertiser.example/offer"
  title="Roasted by veterans, for early mornings"
  sponsor="Black Rifle Coffee"
  onLinkClick={trackAdClick}
/>

<NativeAdCarouselCard
  layout="app"
  media={{ type: 'video', src: creative.mp4, poster: creative.poster, duration: '10:00' }}
  href="https://advertiser.example/offer"
  sponsor="Black Rifle Coffee"
/>
```

<Canvas of={CardStories.Default} />

<Controls of={CardStories.Default} />

The card fills the width of its slot (Figma draws it at 320px for web and 290px for app). In a carousel, give every slot the same width and let the cards stretch: the text block grows so the CTAs line up.

---

## Layouts: one component or two?

The two Figma sets share the same parts (creative, title, Avatar + sponsored label, Button) but not the same anatomy, so they are one component with a `layout` prop:

| | `layout="web"` (12761:19853) | `layout="app"` (12761:19950) |
|---|---|---|
| Surface | none, media flush with the card edge | gradient surface, media and texts inset 12px |
| Media | 320 × 175 | 16:9 |
| Title | `title/500/md`, unclamped | `title/500/md`, 2 lines |
| Sponsored label | `body/400/sm` | `body/400/xsm` |
| CTA | 46px tall | 38px tall |
| Video controls | revealed on hover / focus | always visible |
| States | default · hovered · pressed · focused · disabled | default · pressed (drawn as default) · disabled |

<Canvas of={CardStories.Layouts} />

---

## Media

Figma `Media`: **image** or **video**. The creative is a plain `<img>` / `<video>` (Figma nests `Thumbnail`, which lives outside the Article section). It is decorative unless you pass `alt`, because the title and sponsor already describe the ad.

Videos are always muted at first and have no native controls. On **web** the video plays while the card is hovered or focused; on **app** it plays on its own. With *reduced motion* it never starts and the poster stays. The duration badge (`Badge` translucid · sm) and the mute toggle (`IconButton` translucid · md, `aria-pressed`) sit on the creative.

<Canvas of={CardStories.Media} />

---

## Call to action

Figma `Show CTA`. The CTA is drawn like `Button` (secondary · md · radius sm) but it is **not a second control**: it does the same thing as the card, so it is a presentational `<span aria-hidden="true">` and its text is added to the link's accessible name (the visible "Shop now" stays speakable for voice control).

<Canvas of={CardStories.WithoutCta} />

When the sponsored label runs long, it wraps to 2 lines and then truncates (Figma note "When there are 2 lines of metadata"):

<Canvas of={CardStories.LongMetadata} />

---

## Inspect

Pick layout, media, state and CTA. The card is drawn at 1.25× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={CardStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every Figma variant. Hover, pressed and focus are forced with `data-state` for review.

- **hovered** (web): the image dims and shows "Visit site"; a video reveals its mute toggle and duration.
- **pressed** (web): the creative dims less and the title turns subtlest. Figma's app `pressed` is drawn identical to `default`, so the app layout does not change.
- **focused**: the standard focus ring around the whole card (`--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, `--focus-ring-offset`); on web the card also takes the page surface.
- **disabled**: the card is drawn at `--opacity-disabled`, the link loses its `href` and is announced as a disabled link.

<Canvas of={CardStories.States} />

A carousel row, with cards of different content heights:

<Canvas of={CardStories.Carousel} />

---

## Accessibility

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| One link | The title `<a>` is the card's only link; its `::after` covers the card, so the creative, byline and CTA are all part of the hit area |
| Sponsored | The visible "Sponsored by" + sponsor name is part of the link's accessible name (`aria-labelledby` = title + sponsored label + CTA); `rel="sponsored"` by default |
| No nested controls | The CTA is presentational; the mute toggle is a sibling of the link, stacked above it, with its own name and `aria-pressed` |
| Heading | The title is an `h3` by default (`headingLevel` 2–6) |
| Focus | One ring around the whole card (`:has(.link:focus-visible)`); hidden web video controls appear on `:focus-within` and on touch screens |
| Disabled | `aria-disabled="true"` on the link, no `href`; mute toggle disabled |
| Motion | Video never auto-starts with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`; overlay and fade transitions are removed |

### Figma values without a token

| Figma | Token used |
|---|---|
| App surface gradient top `#1e2229` (raw) | `--color-background-neutral-subtlest-default` |
| Web hovered overlay `#0a0909` @ 55% | `--color-overlay-neutral-bold-default` (black @ 60%) |
| Web pressed overlay `#0a0909` @ 35% | `--color-overlay-neutral-subtle-default` (black @ 40%) |
| "Visit site" 15px SemiBold `#fafafa`, no text style | `label/600/md` (14px) + `--color-text-always-light` |
| Disabled layer opacity 40% | `--opacity-disabled` (38%) |
| Title line height 22px | `title/500/md` recipe (`--line-height-120`) |

NativeAdRow

components-nativeadrow · ./components/native-ad-row/NativeAdRow.stories.tsx
Native ad row for article feeds (Figma "Native Ad Ep Row" 12761:20029). The Figma `Breakpoint` axis is responsive CSS: below 768px of its own width the row stacks (Mobile), from 768px it is a 1 : 2 grid (Desktop). Accessible pattern: the title link is the only link and its `::after` covers the row; the mute toggle sits outside the link, above it.

# NativeAdRow

A sponsored row for article feeds: creative (image or muted video), title, description and a "Sponsored by …" byline. The whole row is one link.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Native Ad Ep Row` (12761:20029, 20 variants: Media × Breakpoint × State), section "Article" (8413:56868), page "↳ Content".
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { NativeAdRow } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/native-ad-row'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<NativeAdRow
  media={{ type: 'image', src: creative.url }}
  href="https://advertiser.example/offer"
  title="Roasted by veterans, for early mornings"
  description="Black Rifle Coffee Company is a veteran-founded coffee company…"
  sponsor="Black Rifle Coffee"
  onLinkClick={trackAdClick}
/>
```

<Canvas of={RowStories.Default} />

<Controls of={RowStories.Default} />

---

## Breakpoint

The Figma `Breakpoint` axis is **not a prop**: the row measures its own width with a container query.

- **Mobile** (row narrower than 768px, Figma 369px): a 160px 16:9 creative next to the title (`body/400/sm`, 3 lines), then the description (`body/400/sm`, 3 lines) and the byline (`body/400/xsm`, 1 line). Page surface, 16px bottom padding and a 1px bottom divider.
- **Desktop** (768px and wider, Figma 912px): a 1 : 2 grid — creative left (16:9, radius xl), title (`title/400/lg`, 2 lines), description (`body/400/md`, 3 lines) and byline (`body/400/md`, 2 lines) right. No surface, no divider.

tokens.css has no breakpoint tokens; 768px is the tablet limit already used by `Header`.

<Canvas of={RowStories.Responsive} />

---

## Media

Figma `Media`: **image** or **video**. The creative is a plain `<img>` / `<video>` (Figma nests `Thumbnail`, which lives outside the Article section), decorative unless you pass `alt`.
A video runs muted, looped and without native controls, and never starts with *reduced motion* (the poster stays). The duration badge (`Badge` translucid · sm) and the mute toggle (`IconButton` translucid, sm on Mobile and md on Desktop, `aria-pressed`) are always visible.

<Canvas of={RowStories.Media} />

---

## Inspect

Pick media, breakpoint (frame width) and state. The row is drawn at its Figma width with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply at that width, including the container-query rules — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={RowStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

All 20 Figma variants. Hover, pressed and focus are forced with `data-state` for review.

- **hovered**: the title turns `text/neutral/bold/hovered`; an image dims and shows "Visit site" (a video does not).
- **pressed**: the title turns `text/neutral/bold/pressed`; the creative dims more.
- **focused**: the standard focus ring around the row (`--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, `--focus-ring-offset`), the page surface, no divider, radius sm.
- **disabled**: the row at `--opacity-disabled`, title `text/neutral/bold/disabled`; the link loses its `href` and is announced as a disabled link.

<Canvas of={RowStories.States} />

A feed of rows:

<Canvas of={RowStories.Feed} />

---

## Accessibility

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| One link | The title `<a>` is the row's only link; its `::after` covers the row, so creative, description and byline are part of the hit area |
| Sponsored | The visible "Sponsored by" + sponsor name is part of the link's accessible name (`aria-labelledby` = title + sponsored label); `rel="sponsored"` by default |
| No nested controls | The mute toggle is a sibling of the link, stacked above it, with its own name and `aria-pressed`. Two toggles are rendered (sm / md) and the container query displays one; the hidden one is `display: none` |
| Heading | The title is an `h3` by default (`headingLevel` 2–6) |
| Focus | One ring around the whole row (`:has(.link:focus-visible)`) |
| Disabled | `aria-disabled="true"` on the link, no `href`; mute toggle disabled |
| Motion | Video never auto-starts with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`; overlay and color transitions are removed |

---

## Tokens

Every property of `NativeAdRow.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar). Mobile rules first, then the Desktop container query:

<Canvas of={RowStories.Tokens} />

### Figma values without a token

| Figma | Token used |
|---|---|
| Hovered overlay `#0a0909` @ 40% | `--color-overlay-neutral-subtle-default` (black @ 40%) |
| Pressed overlay `#0a0909` @ 60% | `--color-overlay-neutral-bold-default` (black @ 60%) |
| "Visit site" 15px SemiBold `#fafafa`, no text style | `label/600/md` (14px) + `--color-text-always-light` |
| Desktop title 20px Regular / 25px, no text style | `title/400/lg` (20px, `--line-height-120`) |
| Desktop creative radius 15 | `--border-radius-xl` (16) |
| Mobile creative width 160 | `calc(var(--size-2xl) * 4)` |
| Byline gap 6 (unbound) | `--spacing-sm` |
| Disabled layer opacity 40% | `--opacity-disabled` (38%) |

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={RowStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `media` | `{ type: 'image'; src; alt? } \| { type: 'video'; src?; poster?; alt?; duration? }` | — | Creative (Figma `Media`) |
| `href` | `string` | — | Advertiser destination; the whole row is this link |
| `target` | `string` | — | Link target |
| `rel` | `string` | `'sponsored'` | Link relationship |
| `onLinkClick` | `MouseEventHandler<HTMLAnchorElement>` | — | Row link click (ad tracking) |
| `title` | `string` | `'Roasted by veterans, for early mornings'` | Figma `Title` |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5 \| 6` | `3` | Heading element of the title |
| `description` | `string` | Figma text | Description (3 lines) |
| `sponsoredLabel` | `string` | `'Sponsored by'` | First part of the sponsored label (bold) |
| `sponsor` | `string` | `'Black Rifle Coffee'` | Advertiser name (subtlest) |
| `avatar` | `{ type?; src?; initials? }` | `{ type: 'dw-plus' }` | Byline `Avatar` (3xsm, decorative) |
| `hoverLabel` | `string` | `'Visit site'` | Label on the hovered image |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Figma `State=disabled` |
| `muted` / `defaultMuted` | `boolean` | — / `true` | Video sound, controlled / initial |
| `onMutedChange` | `(muted: boolean) => void` | — | Mute toggle callback |
| `muteLabel` | `string` | `'Mute'` | Accessible name of the mute toggle |
| `durationLabel` | `string` | `'Duration'` | Screen-reader prefix of the duration badge |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class on the root |

> Also accepts native `<div>` attributes via spread on the root (`id`, `style`, `data-*`, …). `ref` points to the root `<div>`, which is the container-query container: give it a width from its parent.

./components/navigation-item/NavigationItem.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as NavigationItemStories from './NavigationItem.stories'

<Meta of={NavigationItemStories} />

# NavigationItem

A single entry of a navigation bar (the web header's News, Shows & Movies, Kids…), with an optional leading and trailing icon.
Two types, three sizes, a selected state, and five interaction states (default, hover, pressed, focused, disabled).
Spec from Figma `Navigation item` (7808:44857) on the "↳ Navigation" page: 60 variants.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { NavigationItem } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/navigation-item'

<nav aria-label="Main">
  <NavigationItem href="/news" selected>News</NavigationItem>
  <NavigationItem href="/kids">Kids</NavigationItem>
</nav>
```

The rendered element follows what the item does:

- `type="link"` always renders `<a>`. When `selected`, it gets `aria-current="page"`.
- `type="button"` renders `<button type="button">`. Give it an `href` and it renders `<a>` with the same pill look (`aria-current="page"` when selected), which is what the web header does for its sections.
- A selected `<button>` gets `aria-current="true"`. When the item opens a menu (the default Figma chevron), pass `aria-expanded` / `aria-haspopup` yourself; they are forwarded.

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={NavigationItemStories.Default} />

---

## Types

Figma's `type` axis is the `type` prop:

- **Button** — no surface at rest; a pill fills on hover (`background/neutral/bold/hovered`), press (`…/pressed`) and focus (`…/default`). The label color never changes.
- **Link** — no surface; hover and press switch the label and icon to `text/neutral/bold/hovered`.

<Canvas of={NavigationItemStories.Types} />

---

## Sizes

| Size | Text style | Font size | Right icon | Button type height | Button type padding |
|------|-----------|-----------|------------|--------------------|---------------------|
| `md` | `label/400/md` · `label/600/md` | `--font-size-sm` (14) | `--size-sm` (16) | `--size-xl` (32) | `--spacing-lg` (12) |
| `lg` | `label/400/lg` · `label/600/lg` | `--font-size-md` (16) | `--size-md` (20) | `--size-2xl` (40) | `--spacing-lg` (12) |
| `xl` | `label/400/xl` · `label/600/xl` | `--font-size-lg` (20) | `--size-md` (20) | `--size-3xl` (48) | `--spacing-xl` (16) |

The left icon is `--size-md` (20) on every size. Icon to label gap is `--spacing-xs`. The link type has no padding and hugs its content.

<Canvas of={NavigationItemStories.Sizes} />

---

## Selected

Selection changes the label weight only: `label/400/*` → `label/600/*`. There is no indicator bar or fill.

<Canvas of={NavigationItemStories.Selected} />

---

## With icons

Figma's `Show left icon` and `Show right icon` booleans are the `leadingIcon` and `trailingIcon` props, which take any name from the icon catalog. Figma's default glyphs are `device-tv-old` (left, hidden by default) and `chevron-down` (right, shown by default).

<Canvas of={NavigationItemStories.WithIcons} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus shows the DS ring on both types: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset`. The surface becomes `background/neutral/bold/default`. Corners: pill on the button type, `--border-radius-sm` on the link type.

---

## Disabled

Label and icons switch to `text/neutral/bold/disabled` and `icon/neutral/bold/disabled`. A `<button>` gets the native `disabled` attribute; a link gets `aria-disabled="true"` and `role="link"`, drops its `href` and ignores clicks.

<Canvas of={NavigationItemStories.IsDisabled} />

---

---

## Accessibility

- Put items inside a `<nav aria-label="…">` landmark; mark the current page with `selected`.
- On touch screens the hit area grows to `--touch-target-min` around the item without changing the layout.
- Hover and press transitions are removed under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`.

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={NavigationItemStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `type` | `'button' \| 'link'` | `'button'` | Figma `type`: pill surface or text only. `link` renders `<a>`; `button` renders `<button>`, or `<a>` with `href` |
| `size` | `'md' \| 'lg' \| 'xl'` | `'md'` | 14 / 16 / 20 px text |
| `selected` | `boolean` | `false` | 600 weight + `aria-current` (`page` on `<a>`, `true` on `<button>`) |
| `leadingIcon` | `IconName` | — | Icon before the label (Figma `Show left icon`) |
| `trailingIcon` | `IconName` | — | Icon after the label (Figma `Show right icon`) |
| `href` | `string` | — | Link target |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disabled colors; `disabled` on `<button>`, `aria-disabled` + no `href` on `<a>` |
| `htmlType` | `'button' \| 'submit' \| 'reset'` | `'button'` | Native button type, only when a `<button>` is rendered |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Label |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native attributes via spread (`onClick`, `target`, `rel`, `aria-*`, etc.).

import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as OttButtonStories from './OttButton.stories'

<Meta of={OttButtonStories} />

# OTT Button

> **TV / OTT (10-foot UI).** Legacy button for the TV apps, driven by a remote's D-pad. Do not use it on web or mobile screens; use `Button` there.

Large action button with an optional icon, host picture, trailing icon and watch-progress bar.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Ott_Button` (12235:151805): `Type` Primary / Secondary × `State` Default / Hover / Inactive / Focus.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

> **Legacy source.** The Figma component binds only old `[Old] 2.0 Semantic Color` variables (dark-only, one mode) and uses raw values for padding (22 / 24), gap (16), radius (8), height (72) and type (20 / 22px).
> Mapping used here:
>
> | Figma | Token |
> |---|---|
> | `Background/Light Container` #1f1f1f | `--color-background-neutral-subtlest-default` |
> | `Background/Container` #151515 | `--color-background-neutral-subtle-default` |
> | `Background/Inverse Base` #fff | `--color-background-inverse-bold-default` |
> | `Text/Default/Base` #fff | `--color-text-neutral-bold-default` |
> | `Text/Default/Strong` #8f8f8f | `--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default` |
> | `Text/Default/Subtle` #525252 | `--color-text-neutral-bold-disabled` |
> | `Text/Inverse/Base` #0a0909 | `--color-text-inverse-bold-default` |
> | `Icon/Strong` · `Icon/Solid` · `Icon/Soft` at 30% opacity | `--color-icon-neutral-subtlest-default` · `--color-icon-neutral-subtlest-disabled` · `--color-icon-inverse-subtlest-default` |
> | `Stroke/Default/Strong` #8f8f8f · `Stroke/Default/Subtle` #525252 | `--color-border-neutral-subtlest-default` · `--color-border-neutral-subtle-default` |
> | `Stroke/Default/Accent` (progress) | `--color-border-brand-bold-default` |
> | padding 22 / 24, gap 16, radius 8, height 72 | `--primitive-dimension-dimension-5-5` / `--spacing-3xl`, `--spacing-xl`, `--border-radius-md`, `--primitive-dimension-dimension-18` |
> | icon 32 / 28 | `--size-xl` / `--primitive-dimension-dimension-7` |
> | text 20 SemiBold, 130%, 4% | `--font-size-lg`, `--Semibold`, `--line-height-120`, `--letter-spacing-default` |

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { OttButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/ott-button'
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={OttButtonStories.Default} />

<Canvas of={OttButtonStories.ActionRow} />

---

## Variants

- **Primary** — no fill; text and icon only until focused or hovered.
- **Secondary** — filled surface.

<Canvas of={OttButtonStories.Types} />

### Content

Figma booleans map to props: `Show Text` → `showLabel`, `Show Icon` → `showIcon` (+ `icon`), `Show Right Icon` → `showTrailingIcon` (+ `trailingIcon`), `Show Host` → `hostImageSrc`, `Show Progress Bar` → `progress` (0–1).
Without visible text, pass `aria-label`. The progress bar is decorative; put the progress in the label when it matters.

<Canvas of={OttButtonStories.Content} />

---

## Sizes

One size: 72px tall, icon 32px (Primary) or 28px (Secondary).
Figma's Hover variant also grows the button (Primary padding 24 / 26, icon 35px, text 22px); there are no tokens for those values, so hover changes colors only.

---

## States

| Figma `State` | Web |
|---|---|
| Default | default |
| Hover | `:hover` — inverse surface (white in dark theme) |
| Focus | `:focus-visible` — Figma's 1px border plus the DS focus ring |
| Inactive | `isInactive` — dimmed while the TV focus is elsewhere; still focusable |
| — | `disabled` — same look as Inactive, not focusable |

Hover and focus are forced for review:

<Canvas of={OttButtonStories.States} />

---

## Inspect

Pick type, state and content. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={OttButtonStories.Inspect} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `OttButton.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

<Canvas of={OttButtonStories.Tokens} />

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={OttButtonStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `'primary' \| 'secondary'` | `'primary'` | Figma `Type` |
| `label` | `string` | `'Text'` | Button text, shown in caps (Figma `Text`) |
| `showLabel` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Text` |
| `icon` | `IconName` | `'box-multiple'` | Leading icon (Figma `Default Icon`, "Episodes_and_more") |
| `showIcon` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show Icon` |
| `trailingIcon` | `IconName` | `'lock'` | Trailing icon (Figma `Right Icon`) |
| `showTrailingIcon` | `boolean` | `false` | Figma `Show Right Icon` |
| `hostImageSrc` | `string` | — | Round host picture (Figma `Show Host`) |
| `hostImageAlt` | `string` | `''` | Alt text for the host picture |
| `progress` | `number` | — | Watch progress 0–1; shows the bar (Figma `Show Progress Bar`) |
| `isInactive` | `boolean` | `false` | Figma `State = Inactive` |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables the button |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

const Toggle = () => {
    const [playing, setPlaying] = useState(false)

    return (
        <div style={{ display: 'grid', justifyItems: 'center', gap: 12 }}>
            <Artwork width={320} height={180}>
                <PlayButton
                    size="md"
                    playLabel="Play"
                    pauseLabel="Pause"
                    disabled={false}
                    playing={playing}
                    onClick={() => setPlaying((p) => !p)} />
            </Artwork>
            <span
                style={{ fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)' }}>playing: {String(playing)}</span>
        </div>
    );
};

Every Figma variant: Action × InteractionState. Hover / pressed / focus are forced with `data-state`.
const States = ({ size }) => (
  <div style={{ display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: `auto repeat(${STATES.length}, 1fr)`, gap: '20px 12px', padding: 24, alignItems: 'center', fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)' }}>
    <div />
    {STATES.map((s) => (
      <div key={s} style={{ textAlign: 'center', textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{s}</div>
    ))}
    {ACTIONS.map((action) => (
      <Row key={action}>
        <div style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{action}</div>
        {STATES.map((s) => (
          <Cell key={s}>
            <Artwork width={120} height={120}>
              <PlayButton
                size={size}
                playing={action === 'pause'}
                disabled={s === 'disabled'}
                {...(['hover', 'pressed', 'focus'].includes(s) ? { 'data-state': s } : {})}
              />
            </Artwork>
          </Cell>
        ))}
      </Row>
    ))}
  </div>
);

# Play button

Round, translucent play / pause control laid over video thumbnails and artwork. A single button toggles between **Play** and **Pause**: the glyph and the accessible name follow the `playing` prop.

Spec reviewed against Figma `Play button` (13220:37305, doc frame 13220:37367): Action Play / Pause × InteractionState Default / Hover / Pressed / Focus / Disabled × Size md / sm (20 variants).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark — the control is theme-independent by design (it always sits on imagery).

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { PlayButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/play-button'
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.Default} />

```tsx
const [playing, setPlaying] = useState(false)

<PlayButton playing={playing} onClick={() => setPlaying((p) => !p)} />
```

The accessible name is **"Play"** while paused and **"Pause"** while playing (override with `playLabel` / `pauseLabel`, e.g. "Play episode"). There is no `aria-pressed`: the changing name already conveys the state.

---

## Actions

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.Actions} />

---

## Sizes

**md** 80px with a 32px glyph (padding `--spacing-3xl`), **sm** 56px with a 24px glyph (padding `--spacing-xl`).

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.Sizes} />

---

## Toggle

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.Toggle} />

---

## Interaction

- **Hover** — the translucent fill darkens.
- **Pressed** — same fill as hover; the glyph shrinks by 4px (32 → 28, 24 → 20) and the circle by 4px, as in Figma. A 2px margin keeps the outer footprint so the layout does not move.
- **Focus** — the same ring as `Button`: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, transparent `--focus-ring-offset`.
- **Disabled** — fill stays at rest; the glyph drops to 20% white.

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.IsDisabled} />

---

## Inspect

Pick action, state and size. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every Figma variant — Action × InteractionState (hover, pressed and focus are forced for review). Switch the size in Controls:

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.States} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `PlayButton.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme:

<Canvas of={PlayButtonStories.Tokens} />

> The Figma set uses raw values for the fill (#000 25% / 50%), the shadow (0 4 250 #000 50%) and the background blur (4). Code maps them to the closest existing tokens: `--color-overlay-neutral-subtle-default` (40%), `--color-overlay-neutral-bold-default` (60%), `--shadow-xl`, `--primitive-dimension-dimension-1`.

| `playLabel` | `string` | `'Play'` | Accessible name while paused |
| `pauseLabel` | `string` | `'Pause'` | Accessible name while playing |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables the button |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

import { Meta, Canvas } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'

# PodcastSpotlightCard

Displays a featured podcast episode with an artwork-adaptive gradient background. The gradient top color is extracted from the cover image at runtime via Canvas API and automatically adjusted for dark/light theme.

---

## Preview

<Canvas of={CardStories.Default} />

---

## Anatomy

<CardAnatomy />

---

## Install & import (web)

Published package: **`@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds`** (GitHub Packages). In the consuming app, scope the registry in `.npmrc`:

```ini
@henriquemenezzo:registry=https://npm.pkg.github.com
```

Authenticate with a token that has the `read:packages` scope (do **not** commit the token). Then:

```bash
npm install @henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds
```

Global tokens (once in the app entry, e.g. `main.tsx`):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

Component:

```tsx
import { PodcastSpotlightCard } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/podcast-spotlight-card'
```

The DS `package.json` exposes these paths via the `exports` field. The app should use **React 19** (or a compatible version).

---

## Usage

<PlatformCodeTabs
  web={`import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
import { PodcastSpotlightCard } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/podcast-spotlight-card'

<PodcastSpotlightCard
  artworkSrc="/covers/episode-42.jpg"
  episodeName="Breaking down the latest Supreme Court ruling"
  showName="The Michael Knowles Show"
  publishedDate="FEB 14, 2025"
  episodeDuration="58Min"
  onPlay={() => console.log('play')}
  onDownload={() => console.log('download')}
  onShare={() => console.log('share')}
/>`}
  ios={`import DailyWireDS
import SwiftUI

struct EpisodeView: View {
    let artworkURL: URL
    let dominantColor: Color? // optional; pre-computed by the app

    var body: some View {
        DWPodcastSpotlightCard(
            episodeName: "Breaking down the latest Supreme Court ruling",
            showName: "The Michael Knowles Show",
            publishedDate: "FEB 14, 2025",
            episodeDuration: "58Min",
            dominantColor: dominantColor,
            onPlay: { viewModel.play() },
            onDownload: { viewModel.download() },
            onShare: { viewModel.share() },
            artwork: {
                AsyncImage(url: artworkURL) { $0.resizable() }
                    placeholder: { Color.gray }
            },
            playIcon: { Image(systemName: "play.fill") },
            downloadIcon: { Image(systemName: "arrow.down.to.line") },
            shareIcon: { Image(systemName: "square.and.arrow.up") }
        )
        .frame(width: 298)
    }
}`}
  android={`import com.dailywire.ds.DWPodcastSpotlightCard
import com.dailywire.ds.DWIconDefaults
import androidx.compose.material3.Icon
import androidx.compose.material.icons.Icons
import androidx.compose.material.icons.filled.PlayArrow
import androidx.compose.material.icons.filled.Download
import androidx.compose.material.icons.filled.Share
import androidx.compose.ui.unit.dp

@Composable
fun EpisodeScreen(artworkUrl: String, dominantColor: Color?) {
    DWPodcastSpotlightCard(
        episodeName = "Breaking down the latest Supreme Court ruling",
        showName = "The Michael Knowles Show",
        publishedDate = "FEB 14, 2025",
        episodeDuration = "58Min",
        dominantColor = dominantColor,
        onPlay = { viewModel.play() },
        onDownload = { viewModel.download() },
        onShare = { viewModel.share() },
        artwork = {
            // Consumer picks the image-loading strategy (Coil / Glide / etc.)
            AsyncImage(url = artworkUrl, contentDescription = null)
        },
        playIcon = {
            Icon(Icons.Filled.PlayArrow, null, tint = DWIconDefaults.iconColor)
        },
        downloadIcon = {
            Icon(Icons.Filled.Download, null, tint = DWIconDefaults.iconColor)
        },
        shareIcon = {
            Icon(Icons.Filled.Share, null, tint = DWIconDefaults.iconColor)
        },
        modifier = Modifier.width(298.dp),
    )
}`}
/>

---

## Adaptive gradient

The card's top background blends the dominant color extracted from the artwork into the semantic background token. On **web**, extraction is automatic — `useDominantColor` uses the Canvas API on a scaled-down copy of the image. On **iOS** and **Android**, the DS intentionally doesn't ship color-extraction code (keeps the package dependency-free); the consumer passes an optional `dominantColor` prop (many backends already return this value pre-computed). When the prop is `nil`/`null`, all platforms fall back to a flat `--color-background-neutral-subtle-default` background.

<TokenTable
  columns={['Spec', 'Dark mode', 'Light mode']}
  rows={[
    ['Color function', 'darken(dominant, 0.30)', 'lighten(dominant, 0.30)'],
    ['Gradient expression', 'linear-gradient(to bottom, adjusted, bg 85%)', 'linear-gradient(to bottom, adjusted, bg 85%)'],
    ['Background token', '--color-background-neutral-subtle-default', '--color-background-neutral-subtle-default'],
    ['Background value', '#151515', '#f0f0f0'],
    ['Fallback (no image)', 'Solid #151515', 'Solid #f0f0f0'],
  ]}
/>

const Controlled = () => {
    const [liked, setLiked] = useState(false)
    const [saved, setSaved] = useState(true)

    return (
        <ShowcaseBox>
            <ReactionButton
                variant="ghost"
                size="sm"
                showLabel
                defaultSelected={false}
                disabled={false}
                reaction="like"
                selected={liked}
                onSelectedChange={setLiked}
                label={String(12 + Number(liked))} />
            <ReactionButton
                variant="ghost"
                size="sm"
                showLabel
                defaultSelected={false}
                disabled={false}
                reaction="saved"
                selected={saved}
                onSelectedChange={setSaved}
                label={String(4 + Number(saved))} />
        </ShowcaseBox>
    );
};

./components/reaction-button/ReactionButton.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as ReactionButtonStories from './ReactionButton.stories'

<Meta of={ReactionButtonStories} />

# Reaction Button

A toggle button that lets people react to a piece of content (like, save). It stacks a reaction icon over an optional count label and switches between an unselected and a selected state.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Reaction Button` (7724:3624), docs frame 8349:32427.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { ReactionButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/reaction-button'
```

---

## Usage

Click to toggle. Uncontrolled with `defaultSelected`, or controlled with `selected` + `onSelectedChange`:

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.Default} />

```tsx
const [liked, setLiked] = useState(false)

<ReactionButton
  reaction="like"
  selected={liked}
  onSelectedChange={setLiked}
  label={String(count + Number(liked))}
/>
```

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.Controlled} />

---

## Types and reactions

Two types (Figma `type`) and two reactions (Figma `reaction`):

- **Ghost** — no border; the pill fill only appears on hover, pressed and focus.
- **Outline** — 1px `border/neutral/subtle` stroke drawn inside the pill, wider side padding (12px vs 8px).
- **Like** — heart. Selected switches to the filled heart in brand red (`icon/brand/bold/*`).
- **Saved** — bookmark. Selected switches to the filled bookmark and keeps the neutral icon color.

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.Variants} />

---

## Sizes

Sizes only change the icon: **sm** 20px (`size-md`), **md** 24px (`size-lg`), **lg** 32px (`size-xl`).
Padding (`spacing-md` / `spacing-lg`), gap (`spacing-sm`) and the label style (`label/400/md`) are the same in every size.

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.Sizes} />

---

## Without label

Figma `Show label` off. The button keeps the accessible name “Like” / “Save”; when the count is shown it is added to the name (“Like, 12”). The pressed state is announced through `aria-pressed`.

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.WithoutLabel} />

---

## Focus

Keyboard focus fills the pill with `background/neutral/bold/default` and shows the shared ring: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a `--focus-ring-offset` gap (Figma draws it as two drop shadows).

---

## Inspect

Pick type, reaction, selection, state, size and label. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that combination — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Every type × reaction × selection in every state (hover, pressed and focus are forced for review), laid out like the Figma docs frame:

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.States} />

---

## Disabled

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.IsDisabled} />

---

## Tokens

Every property of `ReactionButton.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar). Rows under “like selected” only apply to a selected like button:

<Canvas of={ReactionButtonStories.Tokens} />

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={ReactionButtonStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `reaction` | `'like' \| 'saved'` | `'like'` | Figma `reaction`: heart or bookmark |
| `variant` | `'ghost' \| 'outline'` | `'ghost'` | Figma `type` |
| `size` | `'sm' \| 'md' \| 'lg'` | `'sm'` | Icon size 20 / 24 / 32 px |
| `label` | `string` | — | Figma `Label`: the count under the icon |
| `showLabel` | `boolean` | `true` | Figma `Show label` |
| `selected` | `boolean` | — | Controlled selection (`aria-pressed`) |
| `defaultSelected` | `boolean` | `false` | Initial selection when uncontrolled |
| `onSelectedChange` | `(selected: boolean) => void` | — | Called with the next selection on every toggle |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables the button |
| `aria-label` | `string` | `'Like'` / `'Save'` (+ count) | Overrides the accessible name |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.). Calling `event.preventDefault()` in `onClick` cancels the toggle.

# Scroll Button

Floating pill that brings the user back to the latest content, for example in a live chat or a feed.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Scroll Button` (1522:16829).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

> **Legacy source.** Figma binds old variables (`Background/Light Container` #1f1f1f, `Text/Default/Base`, `Bases/Spacings/spacing-sm (8)` / `spacing-lg (16)`, `radius-pill`).
> They are mapped to `--color-background-neutral-subtlest-default`, `--color-text-neutral-bold-default`, `--spacing-md` / `--spacing-xl` and `--border-radius-full`.
> The Figma shadow (4, 4, blur 16, black 40%) and background blur (90) have no token; the button uses `--shadow-lg` and no blur.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { ScrollButton } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/scroll-button'
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={ScrollButtonStories.Default} />

Floating over a list:

<Canvas of={ScrollButtonStories.OverContent} />

---

## Labels

The Figma `Text` prop maps to `label`.

<Canvas of={ScrollButtonStories.Labels} />

---

## Size

One size: 8 / 16 padding with `body/600/sm` text (37px tall). An invisible area extends the touch target to `--touch-target-min` (44px) without changing the pill.

---

## States

Figma draws only the default. Hover, pressed and disabled use the neutral-subtlest family (`--color-background-neutral-subtlest-hovered` / `-pressed` / `-disabled`).
Hover, pressed and focus are forced for review:

<Canvas of={ScrollButtonStories.States} />

### Focus

Keyboard focus shows the DS ring: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset`.

Pick a state. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

const Breakpoints = (args) => (
  <div style={row}>
    {BREAKPOINT_NAMES.map((bp) => (
      <Frame key={bp} width={BREAKPOINTS[bp]} label={`${bp} · ${BREAKPOINTS[bp]}`}>
        <SummaryNews {...args} />
      </Frame>
    ))}
  </div>
);
Statuses story ok
The timeline dot (Figma StatusDot `Status`). By default it follows the position, as Figma draws it (recent, older, stale…); `status` on an item sets it, e.g. `live` for an entry that is still developing.
const Statuses = () => <Frame width={BREAKPOINTS.Desktop}>
    <SummaryNews
        items={SUMMARY_NEWS_STATUSES.map((status, i) => ({
          id: status,
          status,
          href: '#',
          publishDate: ['Just now', '2 hours ago', '3 hours ago', '4 hours ago'][i],
          title: `${status[0].toUpperCase()}${status.slice(1)}: ${FIGMA_ITEMS[i % FIGMA_ITEMS.length].title}`,

</Frame>;

          { id: '2', href: '#', publishDate: new Date('2026-11-05T12:00:00Z'), formatDate: () => '3 hours ago', title: 'Treasury yields climb as investors weigh new inflation data' },

</Frame>;
States story ok
The list has no state of its own; each entry has the LiveUpdateSummary states. One entry per state, forced with `data-state`, at every breakpoint width.
const States = (args) => {
  const items = STATES.map((s, i) => ({
    ...LINKED_ITEMS[i % LINKED_ITEMS.length],
    id: s,
    publishDate: `${s} · ${LINKED_ITEMS[i % LINKED_ITEMS.length].publishDate}`,
    ...(s === 'default' ? {} : { 'data-state': s }),
  }))
  return (
    <div style={row}>
      {BREAKPOINT_NAMES.map((bp) => (
        <Frame key={bp} width={BREAKPOINTS[bp]} label={`${bp} · ${BREAKPOINTS[bp]}`}>
          <SummaryNews {...args} items={items} />
        </Frame>
      ))}
    </div>
  )
};

# SummaryNews

The latest entries of a live coverage, as a timeline: a status dot and a rail line on the leading side of each `LiveUpdateSummary`.
Three Figma breakpoints (Desktop, Tablet, Mobile) that differ in width only, and four dot statuses.
Spec from Figma `Summary news` (9113:51329, doc frame 9131:57788), section "Article" on page "↳ Content". Each row nests a `Live Updates Article Summary` (the DS `LiveUpdateSummary`, which nests `ArticleMetadata`) and a `StatusDot`. StatusDot is not a DS component: the dot is drawn inside this component.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { SummaryNews } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/summary-news'

<SummaryNews
  headingLevel={4}
  items={updates.map((u) => ({
    id: u.id,
    href: `/live/${story.slug}#${u.id}`,
    publishDate: new Date(u.publishedAt),
    formatDate: timeAgo,
    title: u.headline,
  }))}
/>
```

Renders a `<ul role="list">`; each entry is an `<li>` holding the decorative rail and a `LiveUpdateSummary` (`<article>`, headline as `h3` by default).

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.Default} />

---

## Anatomy

| Part | Spec |
|------|------|
| Surface | `background-neutral-bold-default`, `--spacing-lg` (12) above and below the entries |
| Row | Rail (`--size-xs`, 12) + entry, `--spacing-2xl` (20) apart; rows touch, so the rail lines join the dots |
| Status dot | Figma StatusDot · Size sm: a 6px dot (`--primitive-dimension-dimension-1-5`) centered in a 12px box, `--border-radius-full` |
| Rail line | `--border-default` (1px) wide, `border-neutral-bold-default`, from under the dot to the bottom of the row |
| Entry | `LiveUpdateSummary`, `--spacing-2xl` (20) below it |
| Edge fades | Figma "Overlay top / bottom": `--size-md` (20) tall gradients from the surface color to `overlay-neutral-subtlest-default` (transparent), over the entries and ignored by the pointer |

The rail is `aria-hidden`: the date carries the time, the dot only echoes it.

---

## Breakpoints

Figma's `Breakpoint` axis (Desktop 480, Tablet 672, Mobile 361) changes nothing but the width, so the list is fluid and has no container query. Long headlines wrap and the rail grows with them.

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.Breakpoints} />

---

## Status

Figma draws every dot as StatusDot `recent` and overrides the fill per row: bold, then subtlest, then subtlest disabled. Those are StatusDot's `recent`, `older` and `stale` colors, so the dot defaults to that sequence by position (1st recent, 2nd older, the rest stale). Set `status` on an item to choose it; `live` is the red dot of a developing entry.

| Status | Dot |
|---|---|
| `live` | `icon-live-bold-default` |
| `recent` | `icon-neutral-bold-default` |
| `older` | `icon-neutral-subtlest-default` |
| `stale` | `icon-neutral-subtlest-disabled` |

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.Statuses} />

---

## Links and data

Each item takes the `LiveUpdateSummary` props (`title`, `href`, `publishDate`, `dateTime`, `formatDate`, `linkProps`, `metadataProps`) plus `id` (React key) and `status`. With `href`, the entry is one link stretched over its row text. Without, it is static text, as Figma draws it:

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.Static} />

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.WithData} />

---

## Inspect

Pick a breakpoint. The list is drawn at that Figma width with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply. Hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

The list has no state of its own. Each entry has the `LiveUpdateSummary` states (date and headline turn hovered or pressed together; focus rings the headline), shown here forced with `data-state`:

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.States} />

## Tokens

Every property of `SummaryNews.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar). The entries use the `LiveUpdateSummary` tokens.

<Canvas of={SummaryNewsStories.Tokens} />

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={SummaryNewsStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `items` | `SummaryNewsItem[]` | Figma's three entries | Newest first. `LiveUpdateSummary` props + `id?` + `status?` |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5 \| 6` | `3` | Heading element of every headline |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class on the root |

`SummaryNewsItem.status`: `'live' \| 'recent' \| 'older' \| 'stale'`, default by position.

> Also accepts native `<ul>` attributes via spread (`id`, `aria-*`, `data-*`, etc.). Name the list with `aria-label` when nothing labels it.

const States = () => (
  <div style={{ display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: `auto repeat(${STATES.length}, 1fr)`, gap: '20px 12px', padding: 24, alignItems: 'center', fontSize: 12, color: 'var(--color-text-neutral-subtlest-default)' }}>

        <div style={{ textTransform: 'capitalize' }}>{content}</div>
        {STATES.map((s) => (
          <Cell key={s}>
            <SwipeAction
              label="Remove"
              icon={content === 'icon + label' ? 'trash' : undefined}
              disabled={s === 'disabled'}

import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as SwipeActionStories from './SwipeAction.stories'

# Swipe Action

The action revealed behind a list row when the user swipes it (for example **Remove**).
This component is only the button; the swipe gesture belongs to the list that hosts it.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Swipe Action` (99:8040, Section 1 · 13898:9752).
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

> **Legacy source.** Figma binds the old `[Old] 2.0 Semantic Color` variable `Background/Accent` (#db3d3b) and raw text values.
> They are mapped to the closest DS tokens: brand bold background, `--font-size-xs`, `--Semibold`, `--line-height-120`.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { SwipeAction } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/swipe-action'
```

---

## Usage

<Canvas of={SwipeActionStories.Default} />

In context — the action sits at the end of a row and takes the row's height:

<Canvas of={SwipeActionStories.RevealedInRow} />

---

## Content

Figma draws a caps label only. Its vertical auto layout (8px gap) also takes an optional icon above the label.

<Canvas of={SwipeActionStories.Content} />

---

## Size

One size. Figma is 92 × 92; there is no 92px token, so the button uses `--primitive-dimension-dimension-24` (96px) as minimum width and height and grows with the row.

---

## States

Figma draws only the default. Hover, pressed and disabled use the brand-bold family (`--color-background-brand-bold-hovered` / `-pressed` / `-disabled`), like Button's brand variant.
Hover, pressed and focus are forced for review:

<Canvas of={SwipeActionStories.States} />

### Focus

Keyboard focus shows the DS ring: `--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, with a transparent `--focus-ring-offset`.
If the row clips its overflow, leave room for the ring.

---

## Inspect

Pick the state and content. The button is drawn at 2× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply in that state — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={SwipeActionStories.Inspect} />

|------|------|---------|-------------|

| `icon` | `IconName` | — | Optional icon above the label |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | `false` | Disables the action |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class |

> Also accepts native `<button>` attributes via spread (`onClick`, `aria-*`, etc.).

./components/top-discussions/TopDiscussions.mdx
import { Meta, Canvas, ArgTypes, Controls } from '@storybook/addon-docs/blocks'
import * as DiscussionsStories from './TopDiscussions.stories'

<Meta of={DiscussionsStories} />

# TopDiscussions

A list of discussions, each with a selected image, an optional strip of thumbnails that switches it, and a title that links to the discussion.
Spec reviewed against Figma `Top Discussions` (153:7463, axis `Additional Images` Yes | No), group "Featured Article" (8413:56894) of section "Article" (8413:56868), page "↳ Content". The Figma doc marks it **In Progress**.
All visual values come from tokens in `styles/tokens.css`. Use the toolbar theme switcher to preview Light/Dark.

---

## Import

Import design tokens **once** in the app entry (`main.tsx` or equivalent):

```ts
import '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/styles/tokens.css'
```

```tsx
import { TopDiscussions } from '@henriquemenezzo/dailywire-ds/components/top-discussions'
```

---

## Usage

```tsx
<TopDiscussions
  items={[
    {
      id: 'd1',
      title: 'Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet, consectetur adipiscing elit…',
      href: '/discussions/d1',
      images: [{ src: a, alt: '…' }, { src: b, alt: '…' }, { src: c, alt: '…' }, { src: d, alt: '…' }],
    },
    { id: 'd2', title: '…', href: '/discussions/d2', images: [{ src: e, alt: '…' }] },
  ]}
/>
```

<Canvas of={DiscussionsStories.Default} />

<Controls of={DiscussionsStories.Default} />

Each item is one Figma variant; the list stacks them 16px apart (the spacing of the two variants in the Figma set). Width comes from the parent (Figma 369px).

---

## Additional Images

Figma `Additional Images` is derived from the data: with **two or more images** the thumbnail strip (equal-width thumbnails, 4px apart, 89:46) is drawn 4px above the selected image (369:170); with **one image**, the image alone. The selected thumbnail has a 1px inside stroke. Clicking a thumbnail switches the selected image.

<Canvas of={DiscussionsStories.AdditionalImages} />

The title (`body/400/sm`) wraps to 3 lines, then truncates. Without `href` the item is static.

<Canvas of={DiscussionsStories.Content} />

---

## Inspect

Pick the Figma axis. The list is drawn at 1.25× with its real measurements; the panel lists the tokens that apply — hover a token to see where it acts.

<Canvas of={DiscussionsStories.Inspect} />

---

## States

Top Discussions is static content: Figma draws **no hover, pressed, focus or disabled** states, so none are invented. Keyboard focus uses the DS focus ring (`--color-focus-info-bold-default`, `--focus-ring-width`, `--focus-ring-offset`) on a thumbnail, or around the whole item when its link is focused.
Both variants, with each thumbnail selected:

<Canvas of={DiscussionsStories.States} />

---

## Accessibility

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| List | `<ul aria-label="Top discussions">`, one `<li>` per discussion |
| Link | The title `<a>` is the item's only link; its `::after` covers the item, so the selected image is part of the hit area |
| No nested controls | Thumbnails are `<button aria-pressed>` in a labelled group (`imagesLabel`), stacked above the link, never inside it; names come from `imageLabel(index, total)` ("Show image 2 of 4") |
| Heading | Each title is an `h3` by default (`headingLevel` 2–6) |
| Images | The selected image uses the item's `alt` (decorative when omitted); thumbnail images are decorative (the button carries the name) |
| Touch target | Thumbnails are at least `--touch-target-min` tall |

---

## Tokens

Every property of `TopDiscussions.module.css` with the token it uses, resolved for the active theme (switch Light/Dark in the toolbar):

<Canvas of={DiscussionsStories.Tokens} />

### Legacy Figma variables

This Figma set is still bound to the legacy `[Old] 3.0 Components` and `[Old] 2.0 Semantic Color` variables (dark-only). Each one maps to the current token:

| Figma (legacy) | Value | Token used |
|---|---|---|
| `Top Discussions/Spacing` | 12 | `--spacing-lg` |
| `Top Discussions/Image Spacing` | 4 | `--spacing-xs` |
| `Bases/images-radius` (thumbnails) | 2 | `--primitive-dimension-dimension-0-5` (no 2px semantic radius) |
| `Bases/Corner Radius/radius-xs (4)` (selected image) | 4 | `--border-radius-sm` |
| `Stroke/Default/Base` (selected thumbnail) | white | `--color-border-inverse-bold-default` |
| `Text/Default/Base` (title) | white | `--color-text-neutral-bold-default` |
| Title 14px Regular / 150% / letter spacing 2%, no text style | — | `body/400/sm` (letter spacing `--letter-spacing-default`) |

---

## API Reference

<ArgTypes of={DiscussionsStories} />

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| `items` | `TopDiscussionItem[]` | — | The discussions |
| `headingLevel` | `2 \| 3 \| 4 \| 5 \| 6` | `3` | Heading element of each title |
| `imagesLabel` | `string` | `'Images'` | Accessible name of each thumbnail strip |
| `imageLabel` | `(index, total) => string` | `Show image {index} of {total}` | Accessible name of a thumbnail |
| `aria-label` | `string` | `'Top discussions'` | Accessible name of the list |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class on the list |

`TopDiscussionItem`:

| Field | Type | Description |
|------|------|-------------|
| `id` | `string?` | Stable React key |
| `title` | `string` | Figma "Title" (3 lines) |
| `href` | `string?` | Discussion URL; without it the item is static |
| `images` | `{ src: string; alt?: string }[]` | First selected; 2+ draws the thumbnail strip |
| `defaultSelectedImage` | `number?` | Index selected first (default 0) |

> Also accepts native `<ul>` attributes via spread (`id`, `style`, `data-*`, …). `ref` points to the `<ul>`.
